## `/AST/Effects/Modification/`

### `AttachEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Attach [this permanent] to target [filter]." — the oracle-text instruction to move an Equipment, Aura, or Fortification onto a qualifying object or player. Rule 701.3: "To take an Aura, Equipment, or Fortification from where it currently is and put it onto a specified object or player."

MAST records what oracle text says (the attachment instruction and its target); the rules-engine mechanics of legality checks, zone-change effects, and continuous effects from the attachment are engine territory (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

Distinct from `EquipEffect`, which models the Equip keyword ability (an activated ability defined by Rule 702.6). This effect models explicit oracle-text "attach" instructions, typically inside triggered abilities on Equipment that auto-attach on entry.

- **Effect discriminator:** `attach`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/AttachEffect.cs)

### `BecomesCreatureEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[This permanent] becomes a [P/T] [colors] [subtypes] [card types] creature with [keywords] [until end of turn]." — the Keyrune/Monument "animate" template: a single continuous effect that, for a duration, makes the source permanent into a creature with a fully-specified characteristic set.

There is no single keyword <em>action</em> for "becomes a creature" — the line is one continuous effect (CR 611) that SETS several characteristics of one object at once. The relevant characteristic rules:

<b>CR 205</b> — card types (the added "creature" type) and subtypes (the added "Bird"/"Beast" creature type). <b>CR 208</b> — power and toughness (the stated 2/2, 3/2 box). <b>CR 105</b> — colors (the stated "white and blue" / "red and green"). <b>CR 113.6/702</b> — the granted keyword ability (flying, trample).

<b>Why one composite node rather than a `CompositeEffect` of primitives.</b> MAST is descriptive: it models what the oracle line SAYS, not how a rules engine applies it. The line is grammatically and conceptually ONE thing — "this permanent becomes a [full spec]" — so a single faithful node is truer than a bag of five independent effects (`definePT` + `changeColor` + `changeSubtype` + add-type + `gainAbility`) that would imply five separately-stated effects. The single `Duration` scopes the whole transformation as a unit (it would otherwise have to be replicated onto each child). Reusing `ChangeColorEffect` / `ChangeSubtypeEffect` would also import their replace / Aura-attached semantics, which do not fit an additive animate, and there is no existing "add a card type" primitive at all.

Layer/timestamp ordering (CR 613) — how this continuous effect interacts with others, which layer each characteristic change lands in — is ENGINE territory and is deliberately not modeled here (descriptive-not-executive doctrine).

Examples:

Azorius Keyrune — "This artifact becomes a 2/2 white and blue Bird artifact creature with flying until end of turn." → Subject: Self, Power/ Toughness: 2/2, Colors: ["W","U"], AddedSubtypes: ["Bird"], CardTypes: ["artifact","creature"], GainedAbilities: [flying], Duration: untilEndOfTurn. Gruul Keyrune — "This artifact becomes a 3/2 red and green Beast artifact creature with trample until end of turn." → 3/2, ["R","G"], ["Beast"], ["artifact","creature"], [trample], untilEndOfTurn.

- **Effect discriminator:** `becomesCreature`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/BecomesCreatureEffect.cs)

### `ChangeColorEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"Target [object] becomes [color(s)] until end of turn." — layer-5 (CR 613.1e) color-changing continuous effect. Describes the oracle-text declaration that the named permanent's colors are replaced by the specified set for the duration of the effect.

CR 105.3 (verbatim): "Effects may change an object's color or give a color to a colorless object. If an effect gives an object a new color, the new color replaces all previous colors the object had (unless the effect said the object became that color 'in addition' to its other colors)."

Examples:

Metathran Transport — "{U}: Target creature becomes blue until end of turn." → Colors: ["U"], Duration: untilEndOfTurn

Colors are encoded as one-character color codes matching the oracle-text color name: white = "W", blue = "U", black = "B", red = "R", green = "G", colorless = "C". The "colorless" case is included per CR 105.3 ("give a color to a colorless object") and its inverse (making a colored object colorless), encoded as Colors: [] (empty list) — but for "becomes colorless" the single entry "C" is used for clarity.

MAST is descriptive: this node records what the oracle line says. The rules engine is responsible for how layer-5 color changes interact with other continuous effects (CR 613.7).

- **Effect discriminator:** `changeColor`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ChangeColorEffect.cs)

### `ChangeSubtypeEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"Enchanted [type] is a(n) [Subtype]." — layer-4 (CR 613.1d) subtype-changing continuous effect applied to the attached permanent. Describes the oracle-text declaration that the enchanted permanent's subtypes are replaced by (or set to) the specified set. No Duration: the effect persists while the Aura remains attached (Rule 702.5 / 613.1d).

Examples:

Spreading Seas — "Enchanted land is an Island." → Subtypes: ["Island"] Convincing Mirage — "Enchanted land is a Plains." → Subtypes: ["Plains"] Phantasmal Terrain — various basic land type shapes.

MAST is descriptive: this node records what the oracle line says. The rules engine is responsible for how layer-4 subtype changes interact with other continuous effects (CR 613.7), the implicit basic land mana ability grant (CR 305.6), and landwalk evasion (CR 702.14).

- **Effect discriminator:** `changeSubtype`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ChangeSubtypeEffect.cs)

### `DefinePTEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

A characteristic-defining ability that sets a creature's power and/or toughness to a derived value. Rule 604.3 — the value is equal to some game-state quantity. e.g., "[CardName]'s power is equal to the number of lands you control." e.g., "[CardName]'s power and toughness are each equal to the number of creatures in your graveyard." Distinct from `ModifyPTEffect` which adds to or subtracts from P/T (layer 7c). This effect defines the base value (layer 7a) — it answers "what is the * in the P/T box?" rather than "what modifier is applied?".

- **Effect discriminator:** `definePT`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/DefinePTEffect.cs)

### `ExchangeCharacteristicEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

Exchange a characteristic between two objects. e.g., "exchange text boxes", "exchange power and toughness", "exchange control"

- **Effect discriminator:** `exchangeCharacteristic`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ExchangeCharacteristicEffect.cs)

### `GainAbilityEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[target] gains [ability]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `gainAbility`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/GainAbilityEffect.cs)

### `GainControlEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"gain control of [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `gainControl`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/GainControlEffect.cs)

### `LoseAbilityEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[target] loses [ability]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `loseAbility`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/LoseAbilityEffect.cs)

### `ModifyPTEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"[target] gets +X/+Y" or "gets -X/-Y"

- **Effect discriminator:** `modifyPT`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/ModifyPTEffect.cs)

### `UnattachEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Unattach this permanent." — the oracle-text instruction to remove an Equipment, Aura, or Fortification from the object it is attached to, returning it to the battlefield as an unattached permanent.

CR 702.151a (verbatim): "Reconfigure represents two activated abilities. Reconfigure [cost] means "[Cost]: Attach this permanent to another target creature you control. Activate only as a sorcery" and "[Cost]: Unattach this permanent. Activate only if this permanent is attached to a creature and only as a sorcery."

MAST records what oracle text says (the unattach instruction). The activation condition "only if this permanent is attached to a creature" is a runtime-state guard — no structured node for conditional activation guards exists yet; it is omitted per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine (engine territory, not present as a free-text field per the no-free-text rule).

Distinct from `AttachEffect`, which models explicit "attach" instructions. This effect models the inverse action: detaching a previously attached permanent from its host.

- **Effect discriminator:** `unattach`

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/UnattachEffect.cs)

Characteristics that can be exchanged.

### `PTCharacteristic` — *enum*

Which power/toughness characteristic a `DefinePTEffect` defines.

[Source](AST/Effects/Modification/DefinePTEffect.cs)

### `LegendRuleSuppressionEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"The 'legend rule' doesn't apply." A meta-rule suppression that disables the state-based action described by Rule 704.5j. Rule 704.5j: "If a player controls two or more legendary permanents with the same name, that player chooses one of them, and the rest are put into their owners' graveyards. This is called the 'legend rule.'" Mirror Gallery (MRD) is the canonical card. The effect carries no parameters; its mere presence on a `StaticAbility` records that the legend rule's state-based action is suppressed while the source permanent is on the battlefield.

- **Effect discriminator:** `legendRuleSuppression`

[Source](AST/Effects/Replacement/LegendRuleSuppressionEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Resource/`

### `AddManaEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"add [mana]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `addMana`

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/AddManaEffect.cs)

### `AlternativePaymentEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

A static ability that lets a player pay part of a spell's cost by an alternative means — Convoke (tap creatures), Improvise (tap artifacts), Delve (exile graveyard cards), Assist (another player pays).

CR 702.51b (Convoke), 702.66b (Delve), and 702.126b (Improvise) are explicit that these are <em>neither</em> additional <em>nor</em> alternative costs: they are payment <em>substitutions</em> applied while the spell's normal cost is paid. So this is modeled as an `Effect` on the static ability and never routed to a cost slot (ADR 0003 keyword-decomposition round, HITL-resolved shape).

- **Effect discriminator:** `alternativePayment`

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/AlternativePaymentEffect.cs)

### `ConditionalPayEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"you may pay [cost]. If you do, [effect]" — the conditional-pay triggered pattern. The controller may pay the stated cost; the `IfYouDo` branch carries the consequent effect when payment is made.

Rule 117.3: A player may pay a cost at the time the game asks them to. Rule 603.1: Triggered abilities trigger when their trigger event occurs. The oracle sentence "you may pay [cost]. If you do, [effect]" describes a triggered-ability resolution step in which paying the cost is optional and gated: the consequent fires only if the cost was paid.

This effect is distinct from `UntapEffect { IsOptional = true }` (the Mana Vault shape) in that the payment cost is a first-class field here rather than relegated to the ability's Instructions list. Cards like Deathgreeter ("Whenever a creature dies, you may pay {1}. If you do, you gain 1 life.") have the payment as the descriptive axis — the cost is what the oracle text names, not a gate to an effect that already has its own type. The descriptive-not-engine doctrine (MAST records what oracle text says) requires the cost to appear in the AST.

The `IsOptional` flag is always `true` for this effect: "you may pay" is the canonical form; there is no forced-pay variant at the triggered-ability level modelled by this type.

- **Effect discriminator:** `conditionalPay`

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/ConditionalPayEffect.cs)

### `CostIncreaseEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Cost increase effect: spells matching the containing ability's `AffectedObjects` filter cost more to cast (Rule 601.2f / Rule 117.6 — total cost modification).

Two shapes share this node:

"Noncreature spells cost {1} more to cast." (Thorn of Amethyst / Sphere of Resistance) — the filter sits on the enclosing StaticAbility.AffectedObjects; this effect carries only the Amount. "Spells your opponents cast that target this creature cost {N} more to cast." (pre-Ward pattern) — a targeting condition with CasterFilter and TargetedObject.

- **Effect discriminator:** `costIncrease`

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/CostIncreaseEffect.cs)

### `CostReductionEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Cost reduction effect: reduces the cost to cast this spell. "This spell costs {X} less to cast..." where X is determined by some condition.

- **Effect discriminator:** `costReduction`

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/CostReductionEffect.cs)

### `GainEnergyEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"you get {E}", "you get {E}{E}", etc. — the player gains N energy counters. Rule 107.14: Energy counters are a player resource represented by the {E} symbol; see rule 122 for the generic counter framework. Each {E} symbol in the cost-style notation represents one energy counter. MAST-descriptive: this models the verb + count. Player-state bookkeeping (where the counters live, how they're spent) is engine territory.

- **Effect discriminator:** `gainEnergy`

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/GainEnergyEffect.cs)

### `GainLifeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"gain [amount] life"

- **Effect discriminator:** `gainLife`

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/GainLifeEffect.cs)

### `LoseLifeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"lose [amount] life"

- **Effect discriminator:** `loseLife`

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/LoseLifeEffect.cs)

### `AlternativePaymentKind` — *enum*

What part of the cost an `AlternativePaymentEffect` covers.

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/AlternativePaymentEffect.cs)

### `AlternativePaymentMethod` — *enum*

How an `AlternativePaymentEffect` substitutes payment.

[Source](AST/Effects/Resource/AlternativePaymentEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/Timing/`

### `BecomeMonarchEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"[Player] becomes the monarch." Rule 716 — the monarch designation is a per-game status assigned to exactly one player at a time. MAST records the descriptive instruction; the consequent draw-at-end-step / combat-damage transfer rules are engine territory.

- **Effect discriminator:** `becomeMonarch`

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/BecomeMonarchEffect.cs)

### `CantActivateAbilitiesEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Activation-lock restriction: oracle text states that a permanent's activated abilities "can't be activated." Rule 602.5 — a player can activate an activated ability only when the rules and effects allow it; this effect is a continuous restriction that prevents any activated ability on the named object from being put on the stack.

- **Effect discriminator:** `cantActivateAbilities`

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/CantActivateAbilitiesEffect.cs)

### `CantBeCastEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

A continuous static effect that prevents a class of spells from being cast. e.g. "Noncreature spells with mana value 4 or greater can't be cast." The affected spells are described by the surrounding `AffectedObjects` filter on the containing ability — this effect carries no filter or payload of its own. Rule 601.5 — Legality of casting a spell. A spell that's been put on the stack in violation of this kind of restriction is removed as an illegal action; see also Rule 601.2 (proposing to cast a spell).

- **Effect discriminator:** `cantBeCast`

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/CantBeCastEffect.cs)

### `CastWithoutPayingEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"You may cast [target] without paying its mana cost"

- **Effect discriminator:** `castWithoutPaying`

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/CastWithoutPayingEffect.cs)

### `CommanderDesignationEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"[Card] can be your commander." Used on non-legendary permanents or planeswalkers that can be commanders.

- **Effect discriminator:** `commanderDesignation`

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/CommanderDesignationEffect.cs)

### `OpeningHandEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"If this card is in your opening hand, you may begin the game with it on the battlefield." — the Leyline pattern (Rule 103.6a, opening-hand pre-game placement).

The oracle line describes a conditional pre-game placement: IF the card is in the controller's opening hand, the controller MAY place it onto the battlefield before the game begins. `IsOptional` is true because the oracle text carries "you may".

MAST records this as a descriptive static ability. The conditional ("if this card is in your opening hand") is structural to the ability — all printed Leylines use identical phrasing — so no separate Condition field is needed; the presence of this effect type implies the opening-hand condition.

Rule context: Rule 103.6a governs the mechanic. Rule 103.5 (mulligan) runs before 103.6 actions, so the opening hand is fully resolved before this placement option is offered.

- **Effect discriminator:** `openingHand`

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/OpeningHandEffect.cs)

### `SkipUntapEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Untap-step opt-out: oracle text states "You may choose not to untap this {permanent} during your untap step." Rule 302.6 (untap step — active player determines which of their tapped permanents untap; replacement effects and "doesn't untap" / "may choose not to untap" instructions modify that set). See also Rule 116 (player actions) for the general framework of optional player choices during turn-based actions.

- **Effect discriminator:** `skipUntap`

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/SkipUntapEffect.cs)

### `TimingModificationEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

Timing modification effect: changes when spells can be cast or abilities can be activated. Covers: Flash, "only as a sorcery", "any time you could cast an instant", phase restrictions, etc.

- **Effect discriminator:** `timingModification`

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/TimingModificationEffect.cs)

### `TimingModificationType` — *enum*

Whether a timing modification grants or restricts timing.

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/TimingModificationType.cs)

### `TimingWindow` — *enum*

The timing window for casting or activation.

[Source](AST/Effects/Timing/TimingWindow.cs)

"except it has [keyword/ability]" — adds an ability to the copy. Free-text for now; future refinement can carry a structured Ability node.

"copy [target]" or "create a copy of [target]"

"You get an emblem with [abilities]" Rule 114

- **Effect discriminator:** `createEmblem`

### `CreateTokenEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"create [count] [token description]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `createToken`

### `EmblemDefinition` — *sealed record*

Definition of an emblem created by a planeswalker.

[Source](AST/Effects/TokenCopy/EmblemDefinition.cs)

"investigate" — Rule 701.28. Create a Clue token (an artifact with "{2}, Sacrifice this artifact: Draw a card."). MAST records the keyword action; the Clue token is conventionally inferred from rules text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `investigate`

[Source](AST/Effects/TokenCopy/InvestigateEffect.cs)

### `PowerToughnessOverride` — *sealed record* : CopyModification

"except it's [P]/[T]" — overrides the copy's printed power and toughness.

### `TypeAdder` — *sealed record* : CopyModification

## `/AST/Effects/Traits/`

### `IDurativeEffect` — *interface*

Describes an effect whose oracle text carries a duration clause — e.g., "until end of turn", "until your next turn", "as long as you control...". The trait captures the *presence* of such a clause; the actual scoping rules are a runtime concern handled by consumers of the AST.

MAST is not a rules engine — this trait describes what the card text says, not when the effect actually ends in play.

[Source](AST/Effects/Traits/IDurativeEffect.cs)

### `IOptionalEffect` — *interface*

Describes an effect that the controller may choose whether to perform (the "You may..." prefix in oracle text). The optional follow-up `IfYouDo` captures the "If you do, [Y]" continuation pattern where a secondary effect is contingent on the controller having chosen to perform the main effect.

This trait describes *what the card text says*, not how/when the decision is resolved at runtime — MAST is not a rules engine.

[Source](AST/Effects/Traits/IOptionalEffect.cs)

### `IPreventableEffect` — *interface*

Describes an effect whose oracle text carries an "unless [player] pays [cost]" clause — a syntactic pattern where the effect can be prevented by paying a stated cost. Common on cards like Rhystic Study, Mystic Remora, and Ward.

This trait records the *presence and shape* of the unless clause in oracle text. The decision-and-payment workflow is a runtime concern for consumers of the AST — MAST is not a rules engine.

[Source](AST/Effects/Traits/IPreventableEffect.cs)

## `/AST/Effects/ZoneChange/`

### `CounterPlacement` — *sealed record*

Counters placed as part of a zone-change action. Used when exile (or other movement) carries an attached counter-placement clause, e.g. "exile it with three time counters on it".

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/CounterPlacement.cs)

### `DestroyEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"destroy [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `destroy`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/DestroyEffect.cs)

### `ExileEffect` — *sealed record* : ContinuousEffect

"exile [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `exile`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/ExileEffect.cs)

### `MillEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"mill [count] cards"

- **Effect discriminator:** `mill`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/MillEffect.cs)

### `PutOnTopOfLibraryEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Put [target] on top of its owner's library." A zone-change effect that moves the target from its current zone to the top of its owner's library. Rule 701 (general zone-change actions); distinct from ReturnToHandEffect in destination zone. Most commonly appears on blue bounce variants (e.g. Time Ebb, Temporal Spring).

- **Effect discriminator:** `putOnTopOfLibrary`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/PutOnTopOfLibraryEffect.cs)

### `RegenerateEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"regenerate [target]" — keyword action (Rule 701.19). Creates a regeneration shield on the target permanent: the next time it would be destroyed this turn, instead remove all damage from it, tap it, and if it's in combat remove it from combat. MAST records the effect's presence and target; the shield / destruction-replacement semantics are conventionally inferred from the rules (per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine), mirroring the DestroyEffect / PreventDamageEffect patterns.

- **Effect discriminator:** `regenerate`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/RegenerateEffect.cs)

### `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"return [target] to the battlefield"

- **Effect discriminator:** `returnToBattlefield`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/ReturnToBattlefieldEffect.cs)

### `ReturnToHandEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"return [target] to its owner's hand"

- **Effect discriminator:** `returnToHand`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/ReturnToHandEffect.cs)

### `SacrificeEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"sacrifice [target]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `sacrifice`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/SacrificeEffect.cs)

### `SearchLibraryEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"search your library for [filter]"

- **Effect discriminator:** `searchLibrary`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/SearchLibraryEffect.cs)

### `ShuffleEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"shuffle"

- **Effect discriminator:** `shuffle`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/ShuffleEffect.cs)

### `ShuffleIntoLibraryEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"Its owner shuffles [target] into their library." A zone-change effect that moves the target from its current zone into its owner's library, then the owner shuffles their library. Distinct from `PutOnTopOfLibraryEffect` (which places on top without shuffling) and from `ShuffleEffect` (which shuffles a player's library without moving a target object into it).

Most commonly appears as the resolution of the Unravel the Aether pattern — a two-sentence oracle form "Choose target artifact or enchantment. Its owner shuffles it into their library." Rule 701.20 governs zone-change actions; the shuffle is an inherent part of the zone-change (the owner shuffles immediately after the card is placed in the library). Rule 701.19 governs shuffle.

- **Effect discriminator:** `shuffleIntoLibrary`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/ShuffleIntoLibraryEffect.cs)

### `TransformEffect` — *sealed record* : Effect

"transform this creature" / "transform [target]" Describes the transform keyword action (Rule 701.28): a double-faced permanent transforms by rotating to its other face. MAST records the instructed action and the subject; the rules-engine mechanics of which face becomes visible and any triggered "whenever this creature transforms" abilities are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine. Distinct from the Daybound/Nightbound day/night system (`DayNightEffect`): classic Innistrad werewolves carry explicit triggered abilities that instruct "transform this creature"; Daybound/Nightbound cards transform as part of the day/night state-based system and carry no explicit transform instruction in their oracle text.

- **Effect discriminator:** `transform`

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/TransformEffect.cs)

### `SearchDestination` — *enum*

Where cards go after searching.

[Source](AST/Effects/ZoneChange/SearchDestination.cs)

## `/AST/Quantities/`

### `Quantity` — *abstract record*

Base type for numeric quantities that may be literal, variable, or derived.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `QuantityType`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `CalculatedQuantity` — *sealed record* : Quantity

A quantity derived from a calculation or expression. e.g., "half X rounded down", "twice that many", "+2 for each Aura attached".

A calculated quantity carries EITHER a structured `Operand` (a simple "multiply the base count by N") OR a free-text `Expression` for shapes not yet structured. Exactly one is expected; both are optional so each authoring site can use whichever fits.

- **Quantity discriminator:** `calculated`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `CountQuantity` — *sealed record* : Quantity

A quantity that counts objects matching a filter. e.g., "the number of creatures you control".

`CountOf` is a structured `ObjectFilter` rather than a verbatim phrase: counting "lands you control" or "Aura and Equipment attached to it" is the same object-selection semantics the rest of the AST expresses through `ObjectFilter` (card types, subtypes, supertypes, controller, the `AttachedTo` relational axis). Counting <i>counters</i> — a number that is not a count of objects — is a distinct shape carried by `CounterCountQuantity`.

- **Quantity discriminator:** `count`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `CounterCountQuantity` — *sealed record* : Quantity

A quantity that counts <b>counters</b> of a named kind on an object — "for each oil counter on it" (Serum-Core Chimera). Distinct from `CountQuantity`: a counter is not an object, so an `ObjectFilter` cannot express "how many oil counters are on this permanent". The kind of counter (`CounterType`) and the object bearing them (`On`) are the two axes.

- **Quantity discriminator:** `counterCount`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `DerivedQuantity` — *sealed record* : Quantity

A quantity derived from a characteristic of an object. e.g., "equal to its power", "equal to the number of cards in your hand"

- **Quantity discriminator:** `derived`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `KeywordCostPaidCountQuantity` — *sealed record* : Quantity

A quantity equal to the number of times a keyword's repeatable additional cost was paid as the spell was cast — "for each time it was kicked" (Wolfbriar Elemental, keyed on Kicker: CR 702.33d defines the kicked state and 702.33c folds a multikicker cost into a kicker cost). The count sibling of `KeywordCostPaidCondition` (the boolean was-it-paid): this carries the times-paid for a repeatable keyword (Kicker/Multikicker, and prospectively Squad/Replicate).

Reference-not-resolution (ADR 0004): keyed on the producing keyword's typed `KeywordAbility` identity (a linked ability, e.g. CR 702.33e), NOT a variable threaded from the `AdditionalCastCostEffect` producer. Distinct from `CounterCountQuantity` (counters on an object) and `CountQuantity` (objects matching a filter): a times-paid count is neither a counter nor an object count.

- **Quantity discriminator:** `keywordCostPaidCount`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `LiteralQuantity` — *sealed record* : Quantity

A literal numeric value like 1, 2, 3.

- **Quantity discriminator:** `literal`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `UpToQuantity` — *sealed record* : Quantity

A quantity representing "up to N" choices. e.g., "discard up to two cards"

- **Quantity discriminator:** `upTo`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `VariableQuantity` — *sealed record* : Quantity

A variable quantity like X, Y, Z.

- **Quantity discriminator:** `variable`

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

### `DerivedKind` — *enum*

What a derived quantity is based on.

[Source](AST/Quantities/Quantity.cs)

## `/AST/References/`

### `AbilityReference` — *abstract record*

A filter over a class of abilities or spells, keyed on the surviving keyword identity — the <b>reference half</b> of the production/reference duality (ADR 0003). When a keyword decomposes into shared primitives, its identity survives as a typed `KeywordAbility` label; an `AbilityReference` is what another card's effect matches that label on.

Strong Back's "Equip abilities you activate that target enchanted creature" and "Aura spells you cast that target enchanted creature" are both `AbilityReference`s with a controller and an optional target predicate. Per ADR 0003, the identity that survives decomposition must be the same identity a filter matches on — which is why this is keyed on the typed `KeywordAbility` enum rather than a bare string.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `Kind`

[Source](AST/References/AbilityReference.cs)

### `Characteristic` — *abstract record*

A single characteristic constraint on an `ObjectFilter` beyond the structured axes (card type, subtype, color, zone, comparisons). Where a constraint has a first-class home on `ObjectFilter`, use that; this union is for constraints that do not yet — modelled honestly as typed variants rather than the bare strings this field used to carry.

Today it distinguishes a keyword-ability constraint (`KeywordCharacteristic`) from the typed residual (`OtherCharacteristic`) that holds anything not yet structured. The residual is a deferral, not a destination: state predicates (tapped, attacking), negated types/colors, and relational predicates are carved out into their own variants — or routed to existing `ObjectFilter` fields — in follow-up work.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `CharacteristicType`

[Source](AST/References/Characteristic.cs)

### `HistoryPredicate` — *abstract record*

Base type for backward-looking lifecycle predicates that restrict an `ObjectFilter`. Where the rest of `ObjectFilter` describes the object's *current* characteristics, a history predicate describes *something that happened* to or with the object during a window of past time — e.g. "a creature dealt damage by X this turn", "a spell you cast this turn", "a permanent that entered the battlefield this turn".

These predicates are descriptive only — MAST records what the oracle text says happened, not how a runtime engine would track it.

- **Polymorphic base, discriminator property:** `PredicateType`

[Source](AST/References/HistoryPredicate.cs)

### `ActivatedAbilityReference` — *sealed record* : AbilityReference

Matches a class of <b>activated</b> abilities by their surviving keyword identity — Strong Back / LeoninShikari "Equip abilities" (CR 702.6a, the Equip activated ability being filtered).

[Source](AST/References/AbilityReference.cs)

### `Comparison` — *sealed record*

A numeric comparison.

[Source](AST/References/ObjectFilter.cs)

### `CrewedPredicate` — *sealed record* : HistoryPredicate

"[creature] that crewed [vehicle] [timeframe]". e.g. "target nonlegendary creature that crewed it this turn".

[Source](AST/References/HistoryPredicate.cs)

### `DealtDamageByPredicate` — *sealed record* : HistoryPredicate

"[object] dealt damage by [source] [timeframe]". e.g. "a creature dealt damage by Zurgo this turn".

[Source](AST/References/HistoryPredicate.cs)

### `GameTime` — *sealed record*

A point on the game's turn timeline (CR 500-series turn structure) — the shared primitive composed by the three rules objects that reference the clock: time-triggers ("At the beginning of your upkeep"), delayed triggers ("…at the beginning of the next end step"), and duration endpoints ("until end of turn"). A value type, not an effect or ability. See ADR 0002.

[Source](AST/References/GameTime.cs)

### `KeywordCharacteristic` — *sealed record* : Characteristic

The filtered object must have a keyword ability — e.g. "creature with flying", or (inside an evasion `CanBeBlockedBy` filter) "can be blocked only by creatures with flying".

[Source](AST/References/Characteristic.cs)

### `ObjectFilter` — *sealed record*

Describes a filter for selecting objects. e.g., "nontoken creature with flying you control"

[Source](AST/References/ObjectFilter.cs)

### `ObjectReference` — *sealed record*

A reference to an object in the game. e.g., "target creature", "this creature", "it", "you"

[Source](AST/References/ObjectReference.cs)

### `OtherCharacteristic` — *sealed record* : Characteristic, IResidual

Typed residual for a characteristic constraint that does not yet have a structured variant — carries the literal oracle phrase. Use sparingly; prefer a structured variant (or an existing `ObjectFilter` field) when the shape recurs.

[Source](AST/References/Characteristic.cs)

### `OtherHistoryPredicate` — *sealed record* : HistoryPredicate, IResidual

Escape hatch for backward-looking predicates that don't yet have a structured shape — carries only the literal oracle phrase. Use sparingly; prefer a structured concrete predicate when the shape recurs.

[Source](AST/References/HistoryPredicate.cs)

### `SpellReference` — *sealed record* : AbilityReference

Matches a class of <b>spells</b> by a characteristic filter — Strong Back's "Aura spells you cast". The spell's qualifying characteristics live in `Filter` (subtype Aura, controller You).

[Source](AST/References/AbilityReference.cs)

### `ChosenCharacteristicKind` — *enum*

Which "chosen" characteristic an `ChosenCharacteristic` reference points at — the value selected by a "choose a [creature type|color]" effect as this permanent entered (CR 607 linked abilities). Kind-based and implicit: a permanent has at most one chosen creature type and one chosen color.

[Source](AST/References/ObjectFilter.cs)

### `ComparisonOperator` — *enum*

Comparison operators.

[Source](AST/References/ObjectFilter.cs)

### `ControllerFilter` — *enum*

Filter for who controls an object.

[Source](AST/References/ObjectFilter.cs)

### `KeywordAbility` — *enum*

Canonical identity of a Magic keyword ability (CR 702). A structured alternative to the bare keyword strings the AST has historically carried — casing-proof and exhaustively matchable.

This is the typed target of `Ability.KeywordSource` (ADR 0001): every keyword-as-string a producer emits maps to a member here. Members whose default serialization (the member name verbatim) differs from the exact printed string carry `JsonStringEnumMemberNameAttribute` so the serialized JSON round-trips unchanged (e.g. `BattleCry` ⇄ `"Battle cry"`).

Parameterized keywords carry their parameter separately (in the expanded effect / reminder), so only their bare identity appears here: `Affinity` (the "for &lt;type&gt;" lives in the effect), `Champion` (the championed type lives in `ChampionEffect`), `Landcycling` (the land type lives in `TypecyclingEffect.Type`).

[Source](AST/References/KeywordAbility.cs)

### `ObjectReferenceKind` — *enum*

The kind of object reference.

[Source](AST/References/ObjectReference.cs)

### `TimeBoundary` — *enum*

The beginning or end edge of a `TurnPart`.

[Source](AST/References/GameTime.cs)

### `TimeRelation` — *enum*

"this" or "next" occurrence of the referenced point.

[Source](AST/References/GameTime.cs)

### `TurnPart` — *enum*

A phase or step of the turn, plus the coarse whole-turn and whole-combat spans.

[Source](AST/References/GameTime.cs)

### `Zone` — *enum*

Game zones where cards can exist.

[Source](AST/References/ObjectFilter.cs)

## `/AST/Triggers/`

### `TriggerOccurrence` — *abstract record*

What fires a trigger: either a game <b>event</b> (a permanent dies, a spell is cast) or a <b>time</b> (a clock point — "at the beginning of your upkeep"). ADR 0002 pulled the clock points out of the flat `TriggerEvent` enum into `GameTime`; this union is the one slot a printed *or* delayed trigger uses, matching CR 603's when/whenever/at.

Serialised heterogeneously to keep the migration small: an event is a bare enum string (every existing event fixture is unchanged), a time is a bare `GameTime` object. The implicit conversions let producer sites keep writing `Event = TriggerEvent.Attacks` (or a `GameTime`) without wrapping.

[Source](AST/Triggers/TriggerOccurrence.cs)

### `EventOccurrence` — *sealed record* : TriggerOccurrence

A trigger fired by a game event (CR 603 — zone change, combat, cast, …).

[Source](AST/Triggers/TriggerOccurrence.cs)

### `TimeOccurrence` — *sealed record* : TriggerOccurrence

A trigger fired at a clock point (CR 603 "at" — the beginning/end of a phase or step).

[Source](AST/Triggers/TriggerOccurrence.cs)

### `TriggerCondition` — *sealed record*

Represents the trigger condition for a triggered ability. Rule 603

[Source](AST/Triggers/TriggerCondition.cs)

### `TriggerOccurrenceConverter` — *sealed class* : JsonConverter<TriggerOccurrence>

Reads/writes `TriggerOccurrence` heterogeneously: a JSON string is an `EventOccurrence` (the `TriggerEvent` enum), a JSON object is a `TimeOccurrence` (a `GameTime`).

[Source](AST/Triggers/TriggerOccurrence.cs)

### `TriggerEvent` — *enum*

Categories of trigger events.

[Source](AST/Triggers/TriggerCondition.cs)

### `TriggerTiming` — *enum*

The timing word that starts a triggered ability.

[Source](AST/Triggers/TriggerCondition.cs)

## `/Diagnostics/`

### `Diagnostic` — *sealed record*

Represents a diagnostic message from parsing.

[Source](Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs)

### `DiagnosticSeverity` — *enum*

Severity level for diagnostics.

[Source](Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs)

## `/Keywords/`

### `IKeyword` — *interface*

One self-contained keyword. Each implementation bundles the two facts that used to be split across `KeywordDefinitions.cs` (the expansion definition) and `OracleParsers.cs` (the oracle-text combinator) into a single file under `Keywords/Definitions/`, decorated with `KeywordAttribute` for reflection discovery by `KeywordRegistry`.

Splitting keyword knowledge one-file-per-keyword removes the two-hot-file merge-conflict bottleneck: a new keyword (or a batch of them) is a set of new files with no edits to any shared list or Or-chain.

[Source](Keywords/IKeyword.cs)

### `IKeywordExpander` — *interface*

Service for expanding keywords into their full ability subtrees.

[Source](Keywords/KeywordExpander.cs)

### `KeywordAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `IKeyword` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `KeywordRegistry`. Each decorated file contributes one keyword's definition (to the expander) and combinator (to the keyword Or-chain).

<b>Priority convention (range 0–100, default 50, higher = tried first within its `KeywordTier`):</b> the registry folds discovered combinators into the Simple and Parameterized Or-chains ordered by descending priority then ordinal name. Superpower's `.Or` is first-success-wins, so a keyword whose oracle text is a <i>prefix</i> of another must be tried <i>after</i> the longer one — bump the longer/more-specific keyword's priority so it sorts earlier.

<b>60–100</b> — keywords that must precede a sibling whose token prefix they share. Examples: `Partner with [Name]` (must beat bare `Partner`); multi-word `First strike` / `Double strike` before any single-word keyword that could partially consume their first token. Without the bump first-success-wins would let the shorter sibling shadow them. <b>50</b> — default. Non-overlapping keywords whose leading token is unique (the vast majority — Flying, Toxic, Flashback, …). <b>0–40</b> — deliberately-last catch-alls (e.g. a generic `[Type]cycling` matcher that should yield to specific cycling variants first).

The numeric scheme replaces the old source-order convention: ordering used to be implicit in where a combinator appeared in the `SimpleKeyword` / `ParameterizedKeyword` Or-chains. One-file-per-keyword loses that signal, so each keyword now states its priority explicitly when ordering matters.

[Source](Keywords/KeywordAttribute.cs)

### `KeywordDefinition` — *sealed record*

Defines a keyword ability and how it expands to a full ability subtree. Keywords are shorthand as defined in Rules 701 (actions) and 702 (abilities).

[Source](Keywords/KeywordDefinition.cs)

### `KeywordExpander` — *sealed class* : IKeywordExpander

Default implementation of keyword expansion using a registry of definitions.

[Source](Keywords/KeywordExpander.cs)

### `KeywordRegistry` — *static class*

Reflection-discovered registry of one-file-per-keyword `IKeyword` implementations under `Keywords/Definitions/`. Rebuilds the two artifacts a keyword used to contribute across two hot files:

`RegisteredDefinitions` — the expander's definition list (was `KeywordDefinitions.All`). `RegisteredKeywordList` — the keyword Or-chain combinator (was `OracleParsers.KeywordList`).

As of Phase-2 Stage C this registry is the sole source of keyword knowledge: the legacy `KeywordDefinitions` static-property list and the per-keyword `OracleParsers` combinators have been deleted. `CreateDefault` and `OracleParsers.KeywordList` both delegate here directly — no legacy fallback remains.

[Source](Keywords/KeywordRegistry.cs)

### `KeywordCategory` — *enum*

The category of ability that a keyword expands to.

[Source](Keywords/KeywordDefinition.cs)

### `KeywordParameterType` — *enum*

The type of parameter a keyword can have.

[Source](Keywords/KeywordDefinition.cs)

### `KeywordTier` — *enum*

The Or-chain a keyword's combinator folds into, mirroring the legacy `SimpleKeyword` / `ParameterizedKeyword` split. `AnyKeyword` tries `Simple` first, then `Parameterized`.

[Source](Keywords/IKeyword.cs)

## `/Keywords/Definitions/`

### `AffinityKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Affinity for [text]: This spell costs {1} less to cast for each [text] you control. Rule 702.41. The parameter is a free-text type/subtype label (e.g., "artifacts", "Cats", "Plains", "historic permanents"). MAST captures it as a structured `ObjectFilter` on the cost-reduction's per-object axis.

The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.Affinity` (including its inlined `BuildAffinityFilter` helper); the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.Affinity`, with the expansion inlined to avoid reaching back into `KeywordDefinitions`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/AffinityKeyword.cs)

### `AfflictKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Afflict N: Whenever this creature becomes blocked, defending player loses N life. CR 702.130 (verbatim): "Afflict is a triggered ability. 'Afflict N' means 'Whenever this creature becomes blocked, defending player loses N life.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Afflict", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"BecomesBlocked", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ LoseLifeEffect{ Player:{Kind:DefendingPlayer}, Amount:LiteralQuantity(N) } ] }.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/AfflictKeyword.cs)

### `AfterlifeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Afterlife N — a triggered keyword ability. CR 702.135 (verbatim): "Afterlife is a triggered ability. 'Afterlife N' means 'When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, create N 1/1 white and black Spirit creature tokens with flying.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Afterlife", Trigger:{ Timing:When, Event:Dies, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ CreateTokenEffect{ Count:N, Token:{ 1/1, W+B, creature, Spirit, [Flying] } } ] }

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/AfterlifeKeyword.cs)

### `AftermathKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Aftermath: Cast this spell only from your graveyard. Then exile it. Rule 702.128. Found on the bottom half of split cards. MAST records the keyword's presence; the graveyard-only cast restriction and exile-on-resolution mechanics are engine territory.

The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.Aftermath`; the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.Aftermath`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/AftermathKeyword.cs)

### `AmplifyKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Amplify N: As this creature enters, put a +1/+1 counter on it for each [creature type] card you reveal in your hand. Rule 702.37. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the creature-type-reveal and counter-placement semantics are engine territory. Integer-parameterized keyword; mirrors the Bushido/Modular/Backup family.

The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.Amplify` (including its inlined `ParseIntValue` guard); the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.Amplify`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/AmplifyKeyword.cs)

### `AscendKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Ascend: If you control ten or more permanents, you get the city's blessing for the rest of the game. Rule 702.131. Applies to both permanents (Rule 702.131b) and spells (Rule 702.131a). MAST records the keyword's presence; the city's-blessing designation and downstream effects are engine territory.

The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.Ascend`; the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.Ascend`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/AscendKeyword.cs)

### `AssistKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Assist (CR 702.132a): "Assist is a static ability that modifies the rules of paying for the spell with assist (see rules 601.2g-h). If the total cost to cast a spell with assist includes a generic mana component, before you activate mana abilities while casting it, you may choose another player. That player has a chance to activate mana abilities... the player you chose may pay for any amount of the generic mana in the spell's total cost."

Decomposed to `AlternativePaymentEffect` with `DelegatePayment` (another player pays) and `Generic` (generic mana only). Source is null because payment is delegated to a player, not drawn from objects.

Combinator-only keyword — no `KeywordDefinitions.Assist` legacy entry exists; `Definition` returns `null`. `Tier` is `Simple` because no argument follows the keyword token.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/AssistKeyword.cs)

### `AwakenKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Awaken N—[cost]. Rule 702.113. An alternative cost printed on instants and sorceries: "Awaken N—[cost]". If the spell is cast for its awaken cost, it additionally puts N +1/+1 counters on a target land you control and animates that land. MAST records the keyword, the awaken number N, and the alternative cost; the counters-on-land / land-becomes-creature semantics are engine territory (and echoed in reminder text). Combinator-only: no matching `KeywordDefinitions` entry exists in the legacy registry.

Mirrors the Suspend "N—[cost]" shape (`N` + `Cost`). The number/em-dash glue is parsed by private combinators in this file; the trailing cost reuses the shared `ManaCostSymbols` primitive.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/AwakenKeyword.cs)

### `BackupKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Backup N: When this creature enters, put N +1/+1 counters on target creature. If that is another creature, it also gains the non-backup abilities printed below this one until end of turn. Rule 702.165. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the counter placement, ability-grant, and "printed below this one" scoping are engine territory.

The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.Backup` (including its inlined `ParseIntValue` guard); the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.Backup`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BackupKeyword.cs)

### `BandingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Banding: Legacy combat ability (Rule 702.22). Any creatures with banding can attack in a band; the attacking player controls how the defending player assigns damage to the band. MAST records keyword presence; combat-band semantics are engine territory. Reminder text varies but keyword token is uniform.

Combinator-only keyword — no `KeywordDefinitions.Banding` static property exists; `Definition` is `null`. The combinator is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.Banding`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BandingKeyword.cs)

### `BargainKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Bargain: You may sacrifice an artifact, enchantment, or token as you cast this spell. Rule 702.166. MAST records the keyword's presence; the optional-sacrifice additional-cost and "bargained" designation gating conditional effects are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BargainKeyword.cs)

### `BattleCryKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Battle cry: Whenever this creature attacks, each other attacking creature gets +1/+0 until end of turn. CR 702.91a (verbatim): "Battle cry is a triggered ability. 'Battle cry' means 'Whenever this creature attacks, each other attacking creature gets +1/+0 until end of turn.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"BattleCry", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:Attacks, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ ModifyPTEffect{ Target:{Kind:Each, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"], ExcludeSelf:true, Characteristics:[Other("attacking")]}}, PowerModifier:+1, ToughnessModifier:+0, Duration:untilEndOfTurn } ] }. "Other" is encoded as ExcludeSelf=true on the ObjectFilter (CR 109.5, same as Champion/Soulbond). "Attacking" is a state predicate without a first-class ObjectFilter field, carried as an OtherCharacteristic residual per ADR 0001.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BattleCryKeyword.cs)

### `BestowKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Bestow (Rule 702.103a): "Bestow represents a static ability that functions in any zone from which you could play the card it's on. \"Bestow [cost]\" means \"As you cast this spell, you may choose to cast it bestowed. If you do, you pay [cost] rather than its mana cost.\" Casting a spell using its bestow ability follows the rules for paying alternative costs." Rule 702.103b: a spell cast bestowed "becomes an Aura enchantment and gains enchant creature."

Bestow decomposes across two surfaces. The bestow <em>cost</em> is an alternative casting cost — it lives on the card's `AlternativeCostsAttribute` (emitted by `AttributeExtractor`), not in an effect. This static ability carries only the becomes-Aura / gains-enchant-creature <em>mode</em> (702.103b), expressed with the shared `EnchantRestrictionEffect` enchant-creature primitive. The alternative-cast resolution, Aura-mode toggle, and unattach rules are engine territory (descriptive-not-executive doctrine).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BestowKeyword.cs)

### `BlitzKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Blitz [cost]: three abilities per CR 702.152a (the sacrifice-variant sibling of `DashKeyword`).

CR 702.152a (verbatim): "Blitz represents three abilities: two static abilities that function while the card with blitz is on the stack, one of which may create a delayed triggered ability, and a static ability that functions while the object with blitz is on the battlefield. 'Blitz [cost]' means 'You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than its mana cost,' 'If this spell's blitz cost was paid, sacrifice the permanent this spell becomes at the beginning of the next end step,' and 'As long as this permanent's blitz cost was paid, it has haste and \"When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, draw a card.\"' Casting a spell for its blitz cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `BlitzStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns all three abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback but no longer uses the deleted `BlitzEffect` opaque marker: it emits only the PRIMARY alternative-cast static ability ("You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than its mana cost," from hand). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Blitz decomposes into three.

Combinator-only keyword — no `KeywordDefinition` entry exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions.All` list for this keyword.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BlitzKeyword.cs)

### `BloodthirstKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Bloodthirst N: static ability on the permanent that causes it to enter with N +1/+1 counters when the condition is met. CR 702.54a (verbatim): "Bloodthirst is a static ability. 'Bloodthirst N' means 'If an opponent was dealt damage this turn, this permanent enters with N +1/+1 counters on it.'" MAST shape (ADR-0003 decomposition): StaticAbility{ KeywordSource: Bloodthirst, When: AsThisEnters, Condition: OtherCondition{ Text: "an opponent was dealt damage this turn" }, Effects: [ PutCountersEffect{ Target: Self, CounterType: "+1/+1", Count: LiteralQuantity(N) } ] } The condition "an opponent was dealt damage this turn" has no structured node in the current corpus; ConditionParser.Parse falls through to the typed OtherCondition residual (acceptable per ADR 0001 — residual-debt, not unparsed). Template: KickerConditionalEntersWithCountersRule (StaticAbility{When:AsThisEnters, Condition, PutCountersEffect}).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BloodthirstKeyword.cs)

### `BushidoKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Bushido N: Whenever this creature blocks or becomes blocked, it gets +N/+N until end of turn. CR 702.45 (verbatim): "Bushido is a triggered ability. 'Bushido N' means 'Whenever this creature blocks or becomes blocked, it gets +N/+N until end of turn.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Bushido", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"BlocksOrBecomesBlocked", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ ModifyPTEffect{ Target:{Kind:"It"}, PowerModifier:N, ToughnessModifier:N, Duration:untilEndOfTurn } ] }.

Combinator-only: no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions.cs`. `Definition` returns `null`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BushidoKeyword.cs)

### `BuybackKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Buyback [cost]: two static abilities per CR 702.27a.

CR 702.27a (verbatim): "Buyback appears on some instants and sorceries. It represents two static abilities that function while the spell is on the stack. 'Buyback [cost]' means 'You may pay an additional [cost] as you cast this spell' and 'If the buyback cost was paid, put this spell into its owner's hand instead of into that player's graveyard as it resolves.'"

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `BuybackStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns both abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback but no longer uses the deleted `BuybackEffect` opaque marker: it emits only the PRIMARY additional-cast-cost static ability ("You may pay an additional [cost] as you cast this spell"). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Buyback decomposes into two.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/BuybackKeyword.cs)

### `CascadeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Cascade: When you cast this spell, exile cards from the top of your library until you exile a nonland card whose mana value is less than this spell's mana value. You may cast that card without paying its mana cost. Put the exiled cards not cast on the bottom of your library in a random order. Rule 702.85. MAST records keyword presence; the exile-and-cast machinery is engine territory.

Combinator-only: no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions.cs`. `Definition` returns `null`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/CascadeKeyword.cs)

### `ChampionKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Champion a [type]: When this enters the battlefield, sacrifice it unless you exile another creature of the named type you control. When this leaves the battlefield, that card returns. Rule 702.71. MAST records the keyword's presence and the creature type parameter; the sacrifice-unless and return mechanics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ChampionKeyword.cs)

### `ChangelingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Changeling: This card is every creature type. Rule 702.73. MAST records keyword presence; the all-creature-type characteristic-defining ability is engine territory.

Combinator-only: no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions.cs`. `Definition` returns `null`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ChangelingKeyword.cs)

### `ChooseABackgroundKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Choose a Background — Commander Legends: Battle for Baldur's Gate partner variant (Rule 702.124g). Emits a static ability whose effect carries `ChooseABackground`. Combinator-only: no matching `KeywordDefinitions` entry exists in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ChooseABackgroundKeyword.cs)

### `CipherKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Cipher: Then you may exile this spell card encoded on a creature you control. Whenever that creature deals combat damage to a player, its controller may cast a copy of the encoded card without paying its mana cost. Rule 702.99. MAST records the keyword's presence; the encoding, copy-creation, and free-cast mechanics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/CipherKeyword.cs)

### `ConspireKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Conspire: two abilities per CR 702.78a.

CR 702.78a (verbatim): "Conspire is a keyword that represents two abilities... 'Conspire' means 'As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may tap two untapped creatures you control that each share a color with it' and 'When you cast this spell, if its conspire cost was paid, copy it. If the spell has any targets, you may choose new targets for the copy.'"

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `ConspireStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns both abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback: it emits only the PRIMARY additional-cost static ability ("As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may tap two untapped creatures you control that each share a color with it"). The "each share a color with it" relational predicate is modeled via `SharesColorWith` keyed on `{Kind: Self}` (the spell). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Conspire decomposes into two.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ConspireKeyword.cs)

### `ConvergeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Converge — ability word. Effects scale with the number of colors of mana spent to cast the spell. Rule 207.2c. MAST records the ability-word marker; the color-counting and scaled-effect semantics are engine territory. Mirrors Sunburst (Rule 702.44).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ConvergeKeyword.cs)

### `ConvokeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Convoke (CR 702.51a/b): "Convoke is a static ability that functions while the spell with convoke is on the stack. 'Convoke' means 'For each colored mana in this spell's total cost, you may tap an untapped creature of that color you control rather than pay that mana. For each generic mana in this spell's total cost, you may tap an untapped creature you control rather than pay that mana.'" CR 702.51b: "The convoke ability isn't an additional or alternative cost and applies only after the total cost of the spell with convoke is determined." Accordingly, this is modeled as an `AlternativePaymentEffect` on a static ability — NEVER in a cost slot. `Generic` is the shared baseline (one tapped creature pays {1}); `ColorMustMatchMana` = `true` is Convoke's discriminator (a creature may instead pay one mana of its own color — CR 702.51a).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ConvokeKeyword.cs)

### `CrewKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Crew N: Tap any number of other untapped creatures you control with total power N or more — this Vehicle becomes an artifact creature until end of turn. Rule 702.122. Category is Activated because the comp-rules expansion is an activated ability (702.122a), but the oracle-text shorthand reads as a keyword followed by a numeric parameter. MAST records the keyword's presence and cost; the tap-and-animate mechanics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/CrewKeyword.cs)

### `CumulativeUpkeepKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Cumulative upkeep [cost]: At the beginning of your upkeep, put an age counter on this permanent, then sacrifice it unless you pay its upkeep cost for each age counter on it. Rule 702.24. Category is Triggered because the comp-rules expansion is a triggered ability that fires at the beginning of the controller's upkeep. The age-counter-scaling and sacrifice-unless-pay semantics are engine territory — MAST records the keyword's presence and the cost parameter only.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/CumulativeUpkeepKeyword.cs)

### `CyclingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Cycling {cost}: [Cost], Discard this card: Draw a card.

CR 702.29a (verbatim): "Cycling is an activated ability that functions only while the card with cycling is in a player's hand. 'Cycling [cost]' means '[Cost], Discard this card: Draw a card.'"

The hand-only functional restriction is engine territory — MAST does not add a zone-restriction field. The full cost decomposition is recorded: a mana cost plus a `DiscardCost` (this card), and the effect is a `DrawCardsEffect` (draw 1). Combinator-only: no matching `KeywordDefinitions` entry exists in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/CyclingKeyword.cs)

### `DashKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Dash [cost]: three abilities per CR 702.109a.

CR 702.109a (verbatim): "Dash represents three abilities: two static abilities that function while the card with dash is on the stack, one of which may create a delayed triggered ability, and a static ability that functions while the object with dash is on the battlefield. 'Dash [cost]' means 'You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than its mana cost,' 'If this spell's dash cost was paid, return the permanent this spell becomes to its owner's hand at the beginning of the next end step,' and 'As long as this permanent's dash cost was paid, it has haste.' Casting a spell for its dash cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `DashStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns all three abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback but no longer uses the deleted `DashEffect` opaque marker: it emits only the PRIMARY alternative-cast static ability ("You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than its mana cost," from hand). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Dash decomposes into three.

Combinator-only keyword — no `KeywordDefinition` entry exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions.All` list for this keyword.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/DashKeyword.cs)

Delve — a static ability that allows exile of graveyard cards to pay for generic mana while casting the spell.

### `DethroneKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Dethrone: Whenever this creature attacks the player with the most life or tied for most life, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature. CR 702.105a (verbatim): "Dethrone is a triggered ability. 'Dethrone' means 'Whenever this creature attacks the player with the most life or tied for most life, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource: Dethrone, Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:Attacks, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, InterveningIf: OtherCondition{ Text: "the player you attacked has the most life or is tied for most life" }, Effects:[ PutCountersEffect{ Target: Self, CounterType: "+1/+1", Count: LiteralQuantity(1) } ] } The "attacks the player with the most life or tied for most life" condition has no structured node; it is modelled via ConditionParser.Parse(...) which yields a typed OtherCondition residual (IResidual, not IUnparsed — acceptable per ADR 0001).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/DethroneKeyword.cs)

### `EmbalmKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Embalm is an activated ability that functions while the card with embalm is in a graveyard. (CR 702.128a verbatim): "Embalm is an activated ability that functions while the card with embalm is in a graveyard. 'Embalm [cost]' means '[Cost], Exile this card from your graveyard: Create a token that's a copy of this card, except it's white, it has no mana cost, and it's a Zombie in addition to its other types. Activate only as a sorcery.'"

MAST models this as a single `ActivatedAbility` with two costs: the mana cost parameter and an `ExileCost` (this card from graveyard). The effect is a `CreateTokenEffect` with a copy-override `TokenDefinition`: `IsCopy = true`, overrides white (`Colors = ["W"]`) and Zombie subtype added (`Subtypes = ["Zombie"]`). "No mana cost" and "in addition to its other types" are copy-rules engine concerns — only the structured overrides are modelled.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EmbalmKeyword.cs)

### `EntwineKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Entwine [cost]: You may choose all modes of this spell instead of just the number specified. If you do, you pay an additional [the entwine cost].

CR 702.42: "Entwine is a static ability of modal spells (see rule 700.2) that functions while the spell with entwine is on the stack. 'Entwine [cost]' means 'You may choose all modes of this spell instead of just the number specified. If you do, you pay an additional [the entwine cost].'"

Entwine is optional (IsOptional: true) and paid at most once (Repeatable: false) — you either pay it to unlock all modes or you don't. The mode-selection override is engine territory; MAST models only the additional cost.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EntwineKeyword.cs)

'Equip [cost]' means '[Cost]: Attach this permanent to target creature you control. Activate only as a sorcery.'" CR 702.6c: "Equip [quality]" restricts legal targets to a creature with the chosen quality controlled by the activating player. Escalate [cost]: pay this cost for each mode chosen beyond the first. CR 702.120: "Escalate is a static ability of modal spells (see rule 700.2) that functions while the spell with escalate is on the stack. 'Escalate [cost]' means 'For each mode you choose beyond the first as you cast this spell, you pay an additional [cost].'" IsOptional:true — the controller may choose only one mode (paying zero escalate costs). Repeatable:true — the cost is paid once per extra mode chosen (zero, one, or more times). Combinator-only: no KeywordDefinition entry in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EscalateKeyword.cs)

### `EscapeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Escape (Rule 702.138): "Escape—[cost], Exile N other cards from your graveyard." An alternative cost on instants and sorceries that lets the card be cast from the graveyard. The escape cost has two printed halves — a mana cost and the additional cost of exiling a fixed number of other cards from the controller's graveyard. MAST records both halves; the cast-from-graveyard permission is inferred from the rules and captured as the reminder parenthetical. Combinator-only: no `KeywordDefinition` entry exists.

The exile count is part of the COST. The graveyard exile is deliberately recorded as a count (`CardsToExile`), not modelled as a separate `ExileEffect` subtree — MAST describes the cost, it does not execute it.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EscapeKeyword.cs)

### `EternalizeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Eternalize [cost]: activated ability from the graveyard — single ActivatedAbility node.

Activate only as a sorcery.'"

Costs: mana cost + ExileCost (this card from graveyard). Effects: CreateTokenEffect with IsCopy=true and black/4/4/Zombie overrides. Restriction: OnlyAsSorcery. "No mana cost" and "in addition to its other types" are copy-rules handled by the engine.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/EternalizeKeyword.cs)

'Evoke [cost]' means 'You may cast this card by paying [cost] rather than paying its mana cost' and 'When this permanent enters, if its evoke cost was paid, its controller sacrifices it.' Oracle-text parsing is handled by `EvokeStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns both abilities as a list. ### `ExaltedKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Exalted: Whenever a creature you control attacks alone, that creature gets +1/+1 until end of turn. CR 702.83a (verbatim): "Exalted is a triggered ability. 'Exalted' means 'Whenever a creature you control attacks alone, that creature gets +1/+1 until end of turn.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Exalted", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:Attacks, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"], Controller:You, Characteristics:[Other("attacking alone")]} }, Effects:[ ModifyPTEffect{ Target:{Kind:ThatCreature}, PowerModifier:+1, ToughnessModifier:+1, Duration:untilEndOfTurn } ] }. "attacks alone" is a state predicate with no first-class ObjectFilter field, carried as an OtherCharacteristic residual per ADR 0001 (Mentor precedent). The trigger subject is "a creature you control" (not this creature): Exalted triggers off ANY of the controller's creatures attacking alone. "that creature" maps to ObjectReferenceKind.ThatCreature — the creature named by the trigger's Filter.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ExaltedKeyword.cs)

### `FirebendingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Firebending N (Avatar: The Last Airbender). CR 702.189a (verbatim): "Firebending is a triggered ability. 'Firebending N' means 'Whenever this creature attacks, add N {R}. Until end of combat, you don't lose this mana as steps and phases end.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Firebending", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"Attacks", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ AddManaEffect{ Mana: N×"{R}" } ] }. Mirrors Radha, Heir to Keld ("Whenever Radha attacks, add {R}{R}") — the self-attack trigger uses the creature filter, matching the corpus convention. The end-of-combat mana persistence ("you don't lose this mana as steps and phases end") is an exception to the CR 500.4 mana-emptying turn-based action — engine state bookkeeping, not described content; the verbatim clause survives in the reminder text. The added {R} is the keyword's DEFINITIONAL mana, printed only in reminder text, so it does NOT enter color identity (CR 903.4c) — color identity is derived from printed text by `ColorIdentityDeriver`, which never sees it. Variable-value printings ("Firebending X, …") are out of scope.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FirebendingKeyword.cs)

### `FirstStrikeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

First strike: This creature deals combat damage before creatures without first strike. Rule 702.7.

Exemplar of the <b>multi-word simple</b> keyword shape (Stage A template). The combinator matches two sequential word tokens ("First" then "Strike"/"strike") via chained `Keyword(string)` calls. The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.FirstStrike`; the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.FirstStrike`.

<b>Ordering note:</b> multi-word keywords whose first token is a prefix shared with a shorter keyword must sort earlier in the Or-chain (raise `Priority`). "First" has no single-word collision today, so default priority 50 preserves the legacy ordering. The legacy chain placed First strike / Double strike late; because their leading tokens ("First"/"Double") are unique, order among unrelated keywords is immaterial to first-success-wins.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FirstStrikeKeyword.cs)

### `FlankingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Flanking: Whenever this creature becomes blocked by a creature without flanking, the blocking creature gets -1/-1 until end of turn. CR 702.25 (verbatim): "Flanking is a triggered ability that triggers during the declare blockers step. 'Flanking' means 'Whenever this creature becomes blocked by a creature without flanking, the blocking creature gets -1/-1 until end of turn.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Flanking", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"BecomesBlocked", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"], Characteristics:[OtherCharacteristic{Description:"withoutFlanking"}]} }, Effects:[ ModifyPTEffect{ Target:{Kind:ThatCreature}, PowerModifier:-1, ToughnessModifier:-1, Duration:untilEndOfTurn } ] }.

The trigger's Filter encodes the blocker qualification ("by a creature without flanking") — TriggerCondition has a single Filter field and no dedicated By/blocker axis; the blocking-creature qualifier is placed there and noted. The effect target is ObjectReferenceKind.ThatCreature (the creature named by the trigger condition — the blocking creature), not Self/It (those refer to the Flanking creature itself).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/FlankingKeyword.cs)
