### `ImproviseKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Improvise: for each generic mana in this spell's total cost, you may tap an untapped artifact you control rather than pay that mana.

CR 702.126a: "Improvise is a static ability that functions while the spell with improvise is on the stack. 'Improvise' means 'For each generic mana in this spell's total cost, you may tap an untapped artifact you control rather than pay that mana.'"

CR 702.126b: "The improvise ability isn't an additional or alternative cost and applies only after the total cost of the spell with improvise is determined."

MAST models the payment substitution as an `AlternativePaymentEffect` on the static ability (per ADR 0003 keyword-decomposition shape): tap artifacts you control to pay generic mana. "Untapped" is a game-state precondition (engine territory), not a filter axis.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ImproviseKeyword.cs)

### `IncrementKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Increment (CR 702.191a). A triggered keyword ability printed as a bare keyword token: "Whenever you cast a spell, if this permanent is a creature and the amount of mana spent to cast that spell is greater than this creature's power or this creature's toughness, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature." MAST records the keyword's presence; the trigger condition, mana comparison, and counter placement are engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

Simple parameterless keyword — combinator-only (no KeywordDefinition in the legacy registry). Tier: Simple. Mirrors `TrampleKeyword` shape.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/IncrementKeyword.cs)

### `IndestructibleKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Indestructible: This permanent can't be destroyed. Damage and "destroy" effects that would destroy it have no effect. Rule 702.12. MAST records keyword presence; engine-territory semantics are omitted.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/IndestructibleKeyword.cs)

### `InfectKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Infect: This creature deals damage to creatures in the form of -1/-1 counters and to players in the form of poison counters. Rule 702.91. MAST records keyword presence; the damage-redirection semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/InfectKeyword.cs)

### `IngestKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Ingest: Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a player, that player exiles the top card of their library. CR 702.115a (verbatim): "Ingest is a triggered ability. 'Ingest' means 'Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a player, that player exiles the top card of their library.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Ingest", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:DealsCombatDamageToPlayer, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ ExileEffect{ Target:{Kind:Designated, Filter:{CardTypes:["card"], Zone:Library, Characteristics:[Other("top"), Other("that player's")]}} } ] }. "Top" (positional) and "that player's" (ownership back-reference to the damaged player established by the trigger) are predicates that do not yet have first-class ObjectFilter fields; they are carried as OtherCharacteristic residuals per the ADR 0001 free-text doctrine and the Mentor/Flanking convention. The card is Designated (deterministic by position, no choice) rather than Target (no targeting rule keyword in CR 702.115a).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/IngestKeyword.cs)

### `IntimidateKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Intimidate: This creature can't be blocked except by artifact creatures and/or creatures that share a color with it. Rule 702.13. EvasionEffect with CanBeBlockedBy covering the artifact-type and shares-a-color predicates; mirrors Fear (702.36) but substitutes the color-share predicate for the fixed black-color predicate.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/IntimidateKeyword.cs)

### `IslandwalkKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Islandwalk: This creature can't be blocked as long as defending player controls an Island. Rule 702.14 (landwalk variant). MAST records keyword presence as an EvasionEffect with a DefendingPlayerControls condition on Island permanents. No KeywordDefinition — Islandwalk has no entry in KeywordDefinitions.cs (landwalk variants were not added to the definitions registry).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/IslandwalkKeyword.cs)

### `JobSelectKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Job select: When this Equipment enters, create a 1/1 colorless Hero creature token, then attach this to it. Rule 702.182. Found on Equipment cards from the Final Fantasy set. Although mechanically a triggered ability, MAST records it as a keyword marker — same approach as Living weapon (702.77); the ETB trigger, Hero-token creation, and auto-attach semantics are engine territory. Multi-word keyword via sequential Keyword() combinators, mirroring LivingWeapon and BattleCry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/JobSelectKeyword.cs)

### `JumpStartKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Jump-start: You may cast this card from your graveyard by discarding a card in addition to paying its other costs. If you do, this card is exiled as it resolves. Rule 702.133. MAST records the keyword's presence; the graveyard-cast, discard additional cost, and exile-on-resolution machinery are engine territory. Parameterless keyword marker — mirrors Retrace, Rebound. Note: "Jump-start" is a single Word token.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/JumpStartKeyword.cs)

### `KeywordCombinators` — *static class*

Shared combinator primitives for one-file-per-keyword `IKeyword` implementations under `Keywords/Definitions/`. These are lifted from the private helpers in `OracleParsers` so each keyword file can build its combinator without depending on `OracleParsers` internals.

Behaviour-identical to the legacy private helpers: a keyword migrated to a file produces the exact same `StaticAbility` as the combinator it shadows.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/KeywordCombinators.cs)

### `KickerKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Kicker {cost} (CR 702.33): "Kicker is a static ability that functions while the spell with kicker is on the stack. 'Kicker [cost]' means 'You may pay an additional [cost] as you cast this spell.'" It is a static ability, so the combinator emits a `StaticAbility` carrying the shared `AdditionalCastCostEffect` primitive (`IsOptional = true` — "you may pay"; `Repeatable = false` — at most once, distinguishing it from Multikicker's `Repeatable = true`). The linked "if this spell was kicked, ..." resolution (CR 702.33e / 607) is a separate ability on the card and is not modeled here (ADR 0003/0004 describe-not-execute).

Combinator-only keyword — no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions` registry. The `Keyword("Kicker")` word-matcher matches the whole word "Kicker", so the longer word "Multikicker" (CR 702.33c, handled by `MultikickerKeyword`) does not match this combinator.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/KickerKeyword.cs)

### `LearnKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Learn: You may reveal a Lesson card you own from outside the game and put it into your hand, or discard a card to draw a card. Rule 702.148. A keyword action: parameterless keyword marker. MAST records keyword presence; the choice semantics are engine territory. Note: oracle text uses "Learn." (with a trailing period absorbed by the sentence tokenizer) followed by the reminder in parentheses.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/LearnKeyword.cs)

### `LifelinkKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Lifelink: Damage dealt by this creature also causes you to gain that much life. Rule 702.15.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/LifelinkKeyword.cs)

### `LivingWeaponKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Living weapon: When this Equipment enters, create a 0/0 black Phyrexian Germ creature token, then attach this to it. Rule 702.77. Although mechanically a triggered ability, MAST records it as a keyword marker — same approach as Evolve and Flanking; the ETB trigger, token-creation, and auto-attach semantics are engine territory. Multi-word keyword matched by chaining two Keyword() calls ("Living" then "weapon").

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/LivingWeaponKeyword.cs)

### `MadnessKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Madness {cost} (CR 702.35a/702.35b): "Madness [cost]" represents two abilities. The first is a static ability: "If a player would discard this card, that player discards it, but exiles it instead of putting it into their graveyard." The second is a triggered ability: "When this card is exiled this way, its owner may cast it by paying [cost] rather than paying its mana cost." (CR 702.35a verbatim.) Casting uses rules for alternative costs (CR 702.35b → 601.2b, 601.2f-h).

MAST describes, does not execute. The describe axis MAST keeps is the permission to cast from exile at an alternative cost, modeled by the shared `AlternativeCastEffect` primitive (`FromZone = Exile`). The discard-into-exile replacement event and the "when exiled this way" triggered wrapper are engine territory (ADR-0003 describe-not-execute). No `Condition` — Madness is unconditional once the discard occurs.

Combinator-only keyword: Madness has no entry in `KeywordDefinitions.All` (it is not registered as a `KeywordDefinition`), so `Definition` is <see langword="null"/>.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MadnessKeyword.cs)

### `MayhemKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Mayhem {cost}: A static ability that functions while the card is in the graveyard, allowing it to be cast from there by paying the mayhem cost rather than its mana cost, gated on having discarded the card this turn.

CR 702.187a: "Mayhem is a static ability that functions while the card with mayhem is in a player's graveyard."

CR 702.187b: "'Mayhem [cost]' means 'As long as you discarded this card this turn, you may cast it from your graveyard by paying [cost] rather than paying its mana cost.' Casting a spell using its mayhem ability follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Combinator-only keyword: Mayhem has no entry in `KeywordDefinitions.All` (it is not registered as a `KeywordDefinition`), so `Definition` is <see langword="null"/>.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MayhemKeyword.cs)

### `MegamorphKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Megamorph [cost]: A variant of Morph (Rule 702.37b). The player may cast this card face down as a 2/2 colorless creature for {3}, and may turn it face up by paying its megamorph cost; when turned face up via the megamorph cost, a +1/+1 counter is placed on the permanent. MAST records the keyword and the megamorph cost; the cast-face-down rules, turn-face-up mechanics, and counter-placement are engine territory (descriptive-not-engine doctrine). Rule 702.37b.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MegamorphKeyword.cs)

### `MeleeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Melee: Whenever this creature attacks, it gets +1/+1 until end of turn for each opponent you attacked with a creature this combat. CR 702.121a (verbatim): "Melee is a triggered ability. 'Melee' means 'Whenever this creature attacks, it gets +1/+1 until end of turn for each opponent you attacked with a creature this combat.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Melee", Trigger:{ Timing:"Whenever", Event:"Attacks", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ ModifyPTEffect{ Target:{Kind:"It"}, PowerModifier:CalculatedQuantity{Expression:"for each opponent you attacked with a creature this combat"}, ToughnessModifier:CalculatedQuantity{Expression:"for each opponent you attacked with a creature this combat"}, Duration:untilEndOfTurn } ] }. The per-opponent amount is not a simple integer or a CountQuantity (the "opponents attacked" set is a game-state query outside MAST's ObjectFilter scope); it is carried as a CalculatedQuantity{Expression} per the ADR free-text residual doctrine for quantities not yet expressible structurally.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MeleeKeyword.cs)

### `MenaceKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Menace: This creature can't be blocked except by two or more creatures. Rule 702.111. Evasion keyword whose distinguishing feature is a minimum blocker count rather than a characteristic filter on the blockers.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MenaceKeyword.cs)

### `MentorKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Mentor: Whenever this creature attacks, put a +1/+1 counter on target attacking creature with power less than this creature's power. CR 702.134 (verbatim): "Mentor is a triggered ability. 'Mentor' means 'Whenever this creature attacks, put a +1/+1 counter on target attacking creature with power less than this creature's power.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Mentor", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:Attacks, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, Effects:[ PutCountersEffect{ Target:{Kind:Target, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"], Characteristics:[Other("attacking"), Other("with power less than this creature's power")]}}, CounterType:"+1/+1", Count:1 } ] }. "Attacking" and "with power less than this creature's power" are relative/state predicates that do not yet have first-class ObjectFilter fields, so they are carried as OtherCharacteristic residuals per the ADR 0001 free-text doctrine and the existing CantBeBlockedRule convention (CantBeBlockedRule.cs line 168).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MentorKeyword.cs)

### `MiracleKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Miracle {cost}: You may cast this card for its miracle cost when you draw it, if it's the first card you drew this turn. Rule 702.94. MAST records the keyword and the printed alternative cost; the draw-trigger and cast-timing mechanics are conventionally inferred from the rules and captured in the reminder parenthetical.

Combinator-only: no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions.cs`. `Definition` returns `null`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MiracleKeyword.cs)

### `MobilizeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Mobilize N: Whenever this creature attacks, create N tapped and attacking 1/1 red Warrior creature tokens. Sacrifice them at the beginning of the next end step. Rule 702.175 (Tarkir: Dragonstorm). Category is Triggered because the comp-rules expansion is a triggered ability. MAST records the keyword and its integer token-creation count.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/MobilizeKeyword.cs)

### `ModularKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Modular N: two abilities per CR 702.43a.

CR 702.43a (verbatim): "Modular represents both a static ability and a triggered ability. 'Modular N' means 'This permanent enters with N +1/+1 counters on it' and 'When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, you may put a +1/+1 counter on target artifact creature for each +1/+1 counter on this permanent.'"

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `ModularStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns both abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback but no longer uses the deleted `ModularEffect` opaque marker: it emits only the PRIMARY enters-with-counters static ability ("This permanent enters with N +1/+1 counters on it"). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Modular decomposes into two.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ModularKeyword.cs)

Morph {cost}: The player may cast this card face down as a 2/2 colorless creature for {3}, and may turn it face up later by paying its morph cost. Rule 702.37. MAST records the keyword and the morph cost; the cast-face-down rules and turn-face-up mechanics are engine territory.

Mountainwalk: This creature can't be blocked as long as the defending player controls a Mountain. Rule 702.15. A landwalk variant — MAST records the keyword's presence; the blocking restriction is engine territory.

Multikicker {cost}: You may pay an additional {cost} any number of times as you cast this spell.

Emits a `StaticAbility` carrying an `AdditionalCastCostEffect` with `IsOptional:true` ("you may pay") and `Repeatable:true` ("any number of times"), sharing the same primitive as Kicker, Buyback, Replicate, etc.

### `PartnerKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Partner (parameterless): You can have two commanders if both have partner. Rule 702.124. The bare form allows any two Partner commanders to pair up, as opposed to "Partner with [Name]" which binds two specific cards.

Default priority 50 — must be tried <b>after</b> `PartnerWithKeyword` (priority 60): both lead with the "Partner" token; `PartnerWith` backtracks when "with" is absent, leaving this parser to match the bare form.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/PartnerKeyword.cs)

### `PartnerWithKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Partner with [Name]: A pair-binding variant of the Partner keyword. The parameter is the literal name of the paired card (e.g., "Amy Pond"). Rule 702.124. MAST records the keyword and the partner name; the commander-pairing rules are engine territory.

<b>Priority 60</b> — must be tried before `PartnerKeyword` (priority 50): both lead with the "Partner" token; giving this a higher priority ensures the registry tries `Partner with [Name]` first and backtracks to bare `Partner` only when the "with" token is absent.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/PartnerWithKeyword.cs)

### `PersistKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Persist: triggered dies-ability that returns this permanent to the battlefield with a -1/-1 counter, gated by an intervening-if checking for no existing -1/-1 counters. CR 702.79a (verbatim): "Persist is a triggered ability. 'Persist' means 'When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, if it had no -1/-1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner's control with a -1/-1 counter on it.'" MAST shape: TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Persist", Trigger:{ Timing:When, Event:Dies, Filter:{ Kind:Self } }, InterveningIf:{ ConditionType:"other", Text:"it had no -1/-1 counters on it" } (CR 603.4 intervening-if), Effects:[ ReturnToBattlefieldEffect{ Target:{ Kind:Self }, UnderControl:{ Kind:Owner }, WithCounters:{ CounterType:"-1/-1", Count:1 } } ] }

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/PersistKeyword.cs)

Category is Activated because the comp-rules expansion is an activated ability (702.171a), but the oracle-text shorthand reads as a keyword followed by a numeric threshold parameter. ### `ScavengeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Scavenge [cost]: An activated ability that functions only while the card with scavenge is in a graveyard.

CR 702.97a (verbatim): "Scavenge is an activated ability that functions only while the card with scavenge is in a graveyard. 'Scavenge [cost]' means '[Cost], Exile this card from your graveyard: Put a number of +1/+1 counters equal to the power of the card you exiled on target creature. Activate only as a sorcery.'"

MAST models this as a fully decomposed `ActivatedAbility`:

Costs: the printed mana cost + an `ExileCost` (this card, from Graveyard, quantity 1). Effects: a `PutCountersEffect` placing +1/+1 counters on a target creature; the count is a `DerivedQuantity` keyed on `Power` of "the card you exiled". Restrictions: `OnlyAsSorcery`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ScavengeKeyword.cs)

### `ShadowKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Shadow: This creature can't be blocked except by creatures with shadow, and a creature without shadow can't be blocked by creatures with shadow. Rule 702.28. Mutual evasion: only shadow can block shadow. EvasionEffect with CanBeBlockedBy restricted to the "shadow" characteristic.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ShadowKeyword.cs)

### `ShroudKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Shroud: This permanent can't be the target of spells or abilities (including by its controller). Rule 702.18. MAST records keyword presence.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ShroudKeyword.cs)

### `SkulkKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Skulk: This creature can't be blocked by creatures with greater power. Rule 702.116b. An evasion keyword — MAST records the keyword's presence; the power-comparison blocking restriction is engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SkulkKeyword.cs)

### `SneakKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Sneak {cost}: An alternative-cost keyword found on spells. The caster may pay the Sneak cost instead of the regular mana cost if they also return an unblocked attacker they control to hand during the declare blockers step (Rule 702.173). MAST records the keyword and its mana cost; the attacker-return condition is reminder text inferred from rules.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SneakKeyword.cs)

### `SoulbondKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Soulbond: You may pair this creature with another unpaired creature when either enters. They remain paired for as long as you control both of them. Rule 702.95. MAST records the keyword's presence; the pairing mechanics and any granted abilities are engine territory (same approach as Flanking and Evolve).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SoulbondKeyword.cs)

### `SoulshiftKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Soulshift N — a triggered keyword ability. CR 702.46a (verbatim): "Soulshift is a triggered ability. 'Soulshift N' means 'When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, you may return target Spirit card with mana value N or less from your graveyard to your hand.'" "Put into a graveyard from the battlefield" = `Dies` (CR 700.4). MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:Soulshift, Trigger:{ Timing:When, Event:Dies, Filter:{CardTypes:["permanent"]} }, Effects:[ optional(ReturnToHandEffect{ Target:{Kind:Target, Filter:{ CardTypes:["card"], Subtypes:["Spirit"], Zone:Graveyard, Owner:You, ManaValueComparison:{Operator:LessThanOrEqual, Value:N} }} }) ] }

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SoulshiftKeyword.cs)

### `SpectacleKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Spectacle [cost] (CR 702.137a): "Spectacle is a static ability that functions on the stack. 'Spectacle [cost]' means 'You may pay [cost] rather than pay this spell's mana cost if an opponent lost life this turn.' Casting a spell for its spectacle cost follows the rules for paying alternative costs in rules 601.2b and 601.2f-h."

Decomposed to the shared `AlternativeCastEffect` primitive (`FromZone = Hand`): Spectacle is a cast-from-hand conditional alternative cost, matching the Surge/Freerunning family. The opponent-lost-life precondition is held in an `OtherCondition` residual (per the established Echo/Persist pattern).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SpectacleKeyword.cs)

### `SpliceKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Splice onto [subtype] {cost}: while this card is in hand, you may reveal it as you cast a spell of the named subtype and pay the splice cost to graft this card's instructions onto that spell. Rule 702.47.

MAST records the keyword, the spell subtype a spell must share to be a legal splice target (printed verbatim after "onto"), and the splice cost. The text-grafting machinery (reveal, copy instructions onto the target spell) is reminder text and is conventionally inferred from the rules — not modeled. Combinator-only keyword: no `KeywordDefinition` exists in the legacy `KeywordDefinitions` registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SpliceKeyword.cs)

### `SplitSecondKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Split second: As long as this spell is on the stack, players can't cast spells or activate abilities that aren't mana abilities. Rule 702.61. MAST records the keyword's presence; the stack-restriction semantics are engine territory. Multi-word keyword via sequential Keyword() combinators, mirroring FirstStrike and CumulativeUpkeep.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SplitSecondKeyword.cs)

### `SpreeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Spree: Choose one or more additional costs. Rule 702.172. Found on modal spells that require choosing at least one mode and paying its additional cost. MAST records the keyword's presence; the mode-selection, additional-cost payment, and multi-mode resolution mechanics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SpreeKeyword.cs)

### `SquadKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Squad {cost} (CR 702.157a): "As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may pay {cost} any number of times" and "When this creature enters, if its squad cost was paid, create a token that's a copy of it for each time its squad cost was paid." Two linked abilities. This combinator is the single-ability fallback path: it emits only the PRIMARY additional-cost static ability (the cost half). The full two-ability decomposition is produced by `SquadStaticRule` (priority 1001, fires first). Combinator-only: no matching `KeywordDefinitions` entry exists in the legacy registry, so `Definition` is `null`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SquadKeyword.cs)

### `StartYourEnginesKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Start your engines! (Aetherdrift): If you have no speed, it starts at 1. It increases once on each of your turns when an opponent loses life. Max speed is 4. Rule 702.178. MAST records the keyword's presence; the speed initialization and increment semantics are engine territory.

The '!' is silently dropped by the tokenizer, so the combinator matches "Start your engines". Multi-word simple keyword.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/StartYourEnginesKeyword.cs)

### `StormKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Storm: When you cast this spell, copy it for each other spell that was cast before it this turn. You may choose new targets for the copies. Rule 702.40.

Storm is rules-defined as a triggered ability. By the codebase convention of attaching keyword expansions to `StaticAbility` with KeywordSource set, the triggered semantics live in the rules engine, not the AST.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/StormKeyword.cs)

### `SunburstKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Sunburst: This permanent enters with a +1/+1 counter on it for each color of mana spent to cast it. (Non-creature artifacts use charge counters instead.) Rule 702.44. MAST records keyword presence; the color-counting and counter-placement are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SunburstKeyword.cs)

### `SurgeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Surge {cost} (CR 702.117a): "Surge [cost] means 'You may pay [cost] rather than pay this spell's mana cost as you cast this spell if you or one of your teammates has cast another spell this turn.'" It is a static ability, so the combinator emits a `StaticAbility` carrying the shared `AlternativeCastEffect` primitive (`FromZone = Hand`, `Cost =` the surge mana cost, `Condition =` the cast-history requirement as an `OtherCondition` residual). Decomposed per ADR-0003: the opaque `SurgeEffect` marker is replaced by the shared alternative-cast primitive that Flashback, Escape, Madness etc. also use.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SurgeKeyword.cs)

### `SuspendKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Suspend N—[cost] (Rule 702.62). A keyword representing three abilities — a static "Suspend N—[cost]" ability functioning in hand, a triggered upkeep ability removing a time counter, and a triggered ability playing the card when the last time counter is removed. MAST records the keyword plus the printed N (time counters) and cost; the three sub-abilities are inferred from the rules and carried in the `Reminder`. Reuses the existing `SuspendEffect`. Combinator-only: no `KeywordDefinition`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SuspendKeyword.cs)

### `SwampwalkKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Swampwalk: This creature can't be blocked as long as the defending player controls a Swamp. Rule 702.14 (landwalk). MAST records keyword presence; the blocking restriction is engine territory.

Combinator-only keyword (no KeywordDefinition in the legacy registry). The combinator is the inline expansion of the private `OracleParsers.Landwalk("Swampwalk", "Swamp")` call. Tier: Simple.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/SwampwalkKeyword.cs)

### `TotemArmorKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Totem armor (oracle text: "Umbra armor" on some Auras): If enchanted creature would be destroyed, instead remove all damage from it and destroy this Aura. Rule 702.102. The comp-rules name is "totem armor"; MAST stores the keyword using that discriminator. The replacement-effect semantics are engine territory.

Multi-word simple keyword ("Totem" "armor"). The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.TotemArmor`; the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.TotemArmor`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TotemArmorKeyword.cs)

### `ToxicKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Toxic N: Whenever this creature deals combat damage to a player, that player gets N poison counters in addition to the damage. Rule 702.164. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the poison-counter placement is engine territory.

Exemplar of the <b>integer-parameterized</b> keyword shape (Stage A template). The `Definition` is the verbatim former `KeywordDefinitions.Toxic` (including its inlined `ParseIntValue` guard); the `Combinator` is the verbatim former `OracleParsers.Toxic`.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/ToxicKeyword.cs)

### `TrainingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Training: Whenever this creature and at least one other creature with power greater than this creature's power attack, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature. CR 702.149a (verbatim): "Training is a triggered ability. 'Training' means 'Whenever this creature and at least one other creature with power greater than this creature's power attack, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Training", Trigger:{ Timing:Whenever, Event:Attacks, Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, InterveningIf:OtherCondition{ Text:"this creature attacks with at least one other creature with greater power" }, Effects:[ PutCountersEffect{ Target:{Kind:Self}, CounterType:"+1/+1", Count:1 } ] }. "At least one other creature with greater power" is a relative/state predicate with no first-class ObjectFilter field; carried as OtherCondition residual per the ADR 0001 free-text doctrine.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TrainingKeyword.cs)

### `TrampleKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Trample: If this creature would assign enough damage to its blockers to destroy them, you may have it assign the rest of its damage to the defending player or planeswalker. Rule 702.19. MAST records keyword presence; the excess-damage assignment is engine territory.

Simple parameterless keyword — combinator-only (no KeywordDefinition in the legacy registry). Tier: Simple.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TrampleKeyword.cs)

### `TransmuteKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Transmute {cost}: {cost}, Discard this card: Search your library for a card with the same mana value as this card, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle. Activate only as a sorcery. Rule 702.49. MAST records the keyword and its associated mana cost; the inner discard/search/reveal/shuffle structure is described by the rules and left to the engine. Mirrors `CyclingKeyword` — combinator-only, no legacy registry entry needed.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TransmuteKeyword.cs)

### `TributeKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Tribute N: As this creature enters, an opponent of your choice may put N +1/+1 counters on it. If they don't, a triggered ability fires. Rule 702.102. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the opponent-choice, counter-placement, and conditional ETB semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TributeKeyword.cs)

### `TypecyclingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

[Type]cycling [cost]: Discard this card: Search your library for a [Type] card, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle. Rule 702.28 (and the 702.29 landcycling variant). Catch-all parser for the type-cycling family. Recognizes three printed shapes:

single-word land-type variants — Forestcycling, Swampcycling, Plainscycling, etc. (the land type is the prefix before "cycling"); two-word "Basic landcycling {cost}" — searches for a basic land card (Type = "Basic land"); bare "Landcycling {cost}" — searches for any land card (Type = "Land").

MAST records the keyword, the land type, and the cost; the inner search/reveal/shuffle structure is inferred from the rules. Lower priority (40) so specific cycling variants can outrank this catch-all in the registry Or-chain.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/TypecyclingKeyword.cs)

### `UmbraArmorKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Totem armor (oracle text: "Umbra armor"): If enchanted creature would be destroyed, instead remove all damage from it and destroy this Aura. Rule 702.102. The oracle text uses "Umbra armor" while the comp-rules name is "totem armor". MAST records keyword presence using the comp-rules discriminator "Totem armor"; the replacement-effect semantics are engine territory. Multi-word keyword; mirrors LivingWeapon.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/UmbraArmorKeyword.cs)

### `UndyingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Undying (CR 702.93, verbatim): "Undying is a triggered ability. 'Undying' means 'When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner's control with a +1/+1 counter on it.'" MAST shape (ADR 0003 decomposition): TriggeredAbility{ KeywordSource:"Undying", Trigger:{ Timing:"When", Event:"Dies", Filter:{CardTypes:["creature"]} }, InterveningIf:{ ConditionType:"other", Text:"it had no +1/+1 counters on it" }, Effects:[ ReturnToBattlefieldEffect{ Target:{Kind:"It"}, UnderControl:{Kind:"Owner"}, WithCounters:{ CounterType:"+1/+1", Count:{QuantityType:"literal", Value:1} } }] }. Mirror of Persist (CR 702.78) with opposite counter polarity: Persist checks for no -1/-1 counters; Undying checks for no +1/+1 counters. The intervening-if "if it had no +1/+1 counters on it" is a CR 603.4 condition on the triggered ability, not an effect guard.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/UndyingKeyword.cs)

### `UnearthKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Unearth [cost]: Return this card from your graveyard to the battlefield. It gains haste. Exile it at the beginning of the next end step or if it would leave the battlefield. Unearth only as a sorcery. Rule 702.84. Scope: mana-cost parameter (all known printings). MAST records the keyword and its activation cost; the return, haste-grant, and exile-at-end-step semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/UnearthKeyword.cs)

### `UnleashKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Unleash: two static abilities per CR 702.98a.

CR 702.98a (verbatim): "Unleash is a keyword that represents two static abilities. 'Unleash' means 'You may have this permanent enter with an additional +1/+1 counter on it' and 'This permanent can't block as long as it has a +1/+1 counter on it.'"

Oracle-text parsing is handled by `UnleashStaticRule` (priority 1001), which returns both abilities as a list. This keyword file keeps the combinator live as a fallback but no longer emits the opaque `KeywordAbilityEffect{Unleash}` marker: it emits only the PRIMARY enters-with-counter static replacement ability ("You may have this permanent enter with an additional +1/+1 counter on it"). The `Definition` is null because `Expand` can only return a single `Ability` and Unleash decomposes into two.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/UnleashKeyword.cs)

### `VanishingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Vanishing N: This permanent enters with N time counters on it. At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a time counter from it. When the last is removed, sacrifice it. Rule 702.63. MAST records the keyword and its integer value; the counter-removal upkeep trigger and last-counter sacrifice mechanics are engine territory. Mirrors Fading (702.32) but uses time counters and triggers on the last counter rather than inability to remove.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/VanishingKeyword.cs)

### `VigilanceKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Vigilance: Attacking doesn't cause this creature to tap. Rule 702.20.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/VigilanceKeyword.cs)

### `WarpKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Warp [cost]: You may cast this card from your hand for its warp cost. It enters the battlefield tapped. Rule 702.185. An alternative-cast keyword. MAST records the keyword and the warp cost; the alternative-cast and enters-tapped mechanics are engine territory — same approach as Plot (702.170) and Dash (702.109).

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/WarpKeyword.cs)

### `WebSlingingKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Web-slinging [cost]: You may cast this spell by paying [cost] and returning a tapped creature you control to its owner's hand rather than paying its mana cost. Rule 702.188. Scope: mana-cost parameter. The return-a-tapped-creature component of the alt cost is engine territory — MAST records the keyword's presence and cost only, mirroring the Cycling/Evoke pattern. Combinator-only: no matching `KeywordDefinitions` entry exists in the legacy registry.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/WebSlingingKeyword.cs)

### `WitherKeyword` — *sealed class* : IKeyword

Wither: This creature deals damage to creatures in the form of -1/-1 counters. Rule 702.80. MAST records keyword presence; the damage-redirection semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Keywords/Definitions/WitherKeyword.cs)

## `/Parsing/`

### `IAbilityParser` — *interface*

Contract for an ability-kind-specific parser. Each implementation handles one `AbilityKind` and is discovered at startup by `AbilityParserRegistry` via an `OracleAbilityParserAttribute` decoration. Implementations own their own fallback behavior — `Parse` must always return at least one ability node (typically an `UnparsedAbility` on failure) so the orchestrator never needs to special-case missing output.

[Source](Parsing/IAbilityParser.cs)

### `AbilityClassifier` — *sealed class*

Classifies oracle text clauses into ability types based on structural patterns. This pre-classification enables routing to specialized parsers.

[Source](Parsing/AbilityClassifier.cs)

### `AbilityParserRegistry` — *sealed class*

Discovers `IAbilityParser` implementations decorated with `OracleAbilityParserAttribute` and maps each `AbilityKind` to its parser. Discovery runs once on first access and is cached for the process lifetime. Adding a new ability-kind parser means creating a new file with the attribute and the interface implementation; no edits to this registry, to `OracleParser`, or to any other existing file are required. Kinds with no registered parser (today: `Modal`, `Spell`, and the fallback path) resolve to a built-in fallback that produces a generic `UnparsedAbility`.

[Source](Parsing/AbilityParserRegistry.cs)

### `AttributeExtractor` — *sealed partial class*

Extracts card attributes from a CardInputDTO. Handles mana costs, colors, color identity, creature stats, and loyalty.

[Source](Parsing/AttributeExtractor.cs)

### `CardParseMetrics` — *sealed record*

Metrics for card parsing.

[Source](Parsing/CardParser.cs)

### `CardParseResult` — *sealed record*

Result of parsing a complete card.

[Source](Parsing/CardParser.cs)

### `CardParser` — *sealed class*

Main entry point for parsing Magic: The Gathering cards. Orchestrates type line parsing, oracle text parsing, and attribute extraction.

[Source](Parsing/CardParser.cs)

### `ClauseClassification` — *sealed record*

Classification result for an oracle clause.

[Source](Parsing/AbilityClassifier.cs)

### `ClauseSplitter` — *sealed class*

Splits oracle text into individual ability clauses. Handles paragraph breaks, modal structures, level/chapter markers, and loyalty abilities.

[Source](Parsing/ClauseSplitter.cs)

### `LevelStanzaClause` — *sealed record*

One pre-grouped LEVEL N-M stanza from a level-up cluster. Carries the raw P/T strings and the inner-ability sub-clauses; the parser converts these into `LevelStanza` AST nodes.

[Source](Parsing/ClauseSplitter.cs)

### `ManaCostParser` — *sealed partial class*

Parses mana cost strings into a list of ManaSymbol objects. Handles all standard MTG mana symbol formats: - Basic: {W}, {U}, {B}, {R}, {G} - Colorless: {C} - Generic: {0}, {1}, {2}, ..., {16}, etc. - Variable: {X}, {Y}, {Z} - Hybrid: {W/U}, {B/G}, etc. - Hybrid generic: {2/W}, {2/U}, etc. - Phyrexian: {W/P}, {U/P}, {B/P}, {R/P}, {G/P} - Phyrexian hybrid: {W/U/P}, {G/W/P}, etc. - Snow: {S}

[Source](Parsing/ManaCostParser.cs)

### `OracleAbilityParserAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute

Marks a class as the parser for a specific `AbilityKind`. Discovered at startup by `AbilityParserRegistry`. The tagged class must implement `IAbilityParser` and have a public parameterless constructor.

[Source](Parsing/OracleAbilityParserAttribute.cs)

### `OracleClause` — *sealed record*

Represents a clause (ability segment) extracted from oracle text. Each clause typically represents a single ability.

[Source](Parsing/ClauseSplitter.cs)

### `OracleParser` — *sealed class*

Main orchestrator for parsing Magic: The Gathering oracle text. Coordinates the tokenizer, clause splitter, classifier, and the reflection-discovered registry of `IAbilityParser` implementations.

[Source](Parsing/OracleParser.cs)

### `TypeLineParser` — *sealed class*

Parses a card's type line into its structural components. Type lines follow the format: "[Supertypes] [Types] — [Subtypes]" Examples: - "Creature — Elf Druid" → types: [Creature], subtypes: [Elf, Druid] - "Legendary Creature — Squirrel Warrior" → supertypes: [Legendary], types: [Creature], subtypes: [Squirrel, Warrior] - "Artifact" → types: [Artifact] - "Basic Land — Forest" → supertypes: [Basic], types: [Land], subtypes: [Forest] - "Legendary Planeswalker — Daretti" → supertypes: [Legendary], types: [Planeswalker], subtypes: [Daretti]

[Source](Parsing/TypeLineParser.cs)

## `/Parsing/Combinators/`

### `OracleParsers` — *static class*

Keyword-ability parser combinators for Magic: The Gathering oracle text.

As of Phase-2 Stage C this type is a thin facade over `KeywordRegistry`: the per-keyword combinators and the `SimpleKeyword` / `ParameterizedKeyword` Or-chains that used to live here have moved to one-file-per-keyword `IKeyword` implementations under `Keywords/Definitions/`, discovered by the registry. The two members below preserve the public call shape (`OracleParsers.KeywordList(tokens)` from `StaticAbilityParser`) while delegating the actual recognition to the registry.

[Source](Parsing/Combinators/OracleParsers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/`

### `IPrioritizedRuleAttribute` — *interface*

Contract for an attribute that marks a reflection-discovered rule and carries its intra-tier dispatch priority. Shared by every registry-style parser family (spell rules, triggered rules, keywords, …) so a single generic discovery helper (`RuleRegistry`) can rank decorated types without knowing the concrete attribute.

<b>Priority convention (range 0–100, default 50, higher = tried first):</b> higher-priority rules are dispatched before lower-priority siblings. Each rule family documents its own band convention on its concrete attribute; the shared invariant is only that a larger number wins ties by sorting earlier.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/IPrioritizedRuleAttribute.cs)

### `ActivatedAbilityParser` — *sealed partial class* : IAbilityParser

Parses activated abilities of the form "[Cost]: [Effect.]" Handles: - Mana abilities: {T}: Add {G}. - Complex activated abilities: {3}{B}{B}: Creatures you control gain lifelink until end of turn. - Loyalty abilities: +2: Discard up to two cards, then draw that many cards.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Activated`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/ActivatedAbilityParser.cs)

### `FallbackParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Fallback parser that always succeeds by producing an UnparsedAbility. This ensures the parsing pipeline never fails completely. Registered for `Unparsed` in the `AbilityParserRegistry`. Also invoked internally by the other concrete parsers when their `TryParse` step fails.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Unparsed`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/FallbackParser.cs)

### `LevelUpAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Parses Level Up clusters (Rule 711). Consumes a level-up cost clause (one that `ClauseSplitter` has pre-grouped with its LEVEL stanzas on `LevelUpStanzas`), then constructs the cost activated ability + the stanza records.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.LevelUp`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/LevelUpAbilityParser.cs)

### `ModalAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Parser for modal abilities ("Choose one —", "Choose two —", "Choose one or both —"). The clause splitter attaches each option as a child `OracleClause` on `ModalOptions`; this parser interprets the header text to derive a `ModeSelection` and dispatches each option clause back through the classifier+registry to parse the option body.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Modal`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/ModalAbilityParser.cs)

### `RuleRegistry` — *static class*

Generic reflection-based rule discovery, shared by every ability parser that dispatches over a priority-ordered set of one-file-per-rule implementations. Each parser is a thin dispatcher over `Discover{TRule, TAttr}`; adding a new shape is dropping a new `[TAttr]`-decorated `TRule` file with no edits to any shared file — which is what keeps the rule directories free of merge conflicts under parallel batch dispatch.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/RuleRegistry.cs)

### `SagaAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Parses Saga chapter structures (Rule 715). Consumes a saga-preamble clause (one that `ClauseSplitter` has pre-grouped with its chapter bodies on `SagaChapters`), then dispatches each chapter body through the registry so individual chapters land as whatever ability shape best matches their text.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Saga`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/SagaAbilityParser.cs)

### `SpellAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Dispatches spell-ability oracle-text clauses to the priority-ordered set of `ISpellRule` implementations discovered by reflection at construction time. Each rule lives in its own file under `Parsers/Spell/Rules/` and is decorated with `SpellRuleAttribute` (see attribute docs for priority convention). Adding a new shape means dropping a new file in `Rules/` with no edits to any shared file.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Spell`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/SpellAbilityParser.cs)

### `StaticAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Dispatches static-ability oracle-text clauses to the priority-ordered set of `IStaticRule` implementations discovered by reflection at construction time. Each rule lives in its own file under `Parsers/Static/Rules/` and is decorated with `StaticRuleAttribute` (see attribute docs for the order-preserving priority convention). Adding a new shape means dropping a new file in `Rules/` with no edits to any shared file.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Static`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/StaticAbilityParser.cs)

### `TriggeredAbilityParser` — *sealed class* : IAbilityParser

Parser for triggered abilities: "When/Whenever/At [trigger], [effect]" Supports multiple trigger events and effect types using compositional parsing.

- **Ability-parser registration, kind:** `AbilityKind.Triggered`

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/TriggeredAbilityParser.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Activated/`

### `IActivatedCostRule` — *interface*

One activated-ability cost-component recognizer. Dispatched by `ActivatedAbilityParser` in descending `Priority` order over each comma-separated cost component; the first non-null result wins. Each rule self-guards and returns `null` when it doesn't recognise the component.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/IActivatedCostRule.cs)

### `IActivatedEffectRule` — *interface*

One activated-ability effect recognizer. Dispatched by `ActivatedAbilityParser` in descending `Priority` order, then ordinal name for tie-breaking; the first non-null result wins. `effectText` is the post-colon effect fragment (a single sentence when reached via the multi-sentence pre-pass). Each rule self-guards and returns `null` when it doesn't recognise the text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/IActivatedEffectRule.cs)

### `ActivatedCostRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `IActivatedCostRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `ActivatedAbilityParser`. Priorities are migrated order-preserving from the legacy `ParseCosts` chain as `Priority = 1000 - (chain index)`. See `IPrioritizedRuleAttribute` for the shared priority contract (higher = more specific = tried first).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/ActivatedCostRuleAttribute.cs)

### `ActivatedEffectRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `IActivatedEffectRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `ActivatedAbilityParser`. Priorities are migrated order-preserving from the legacy `ParseEffects` chain as `Priority = 1000 - (chain index)`, so an extracted rule keeps the exact relative dispatch order it had as an inline branch. See `IPrioritizedRuleAttribute` for the shared priority contract (higher = more specific = tried first).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/ActivatedEffectRuleAttribute.cs)

### `ActivatedRuleHelpers` — *static class*

Shared utilities used across `IActivatedEffectRule` and `IActivatedCostRule` implementations.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/ActivatedRuleHelpers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/`

### `AdaptEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Adapt N" — e.g. "Adapt 2" (CR 701.46a: "\"Adapt N\" means \"If this permanent has no +1/+1 counters on it, put N +1/+1 counters on it.\"") The trailing parenthetical reminder is stripped by the caller before this rule is invoked.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AdaptEffectRule.cs)

### `AddManaEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Add {mana}" — e.g. "Add {G}", "Add {C}{C}{C}", "Add {W}{U}{B}{R}{G}". Also handles "Add one mana of any color" (Crystal Grotto / Chromatic Lantern shape) where the produced mana is a single choice across all five colors, and "Add one mana of the chosen color" (Coldsteel Heart / Shimmerdrift Vale / Thriving lands) where the produced mana's color is the color chosen as this permanent entered — the consumer side of a CR 607 linked "choose a color".

Per CR 605.1a — "An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn't require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player's mana pool when it resolves, and it's not a loyalty ability." — the enclosing "{T}: Add ..." ability is a mana ability; the chosen-color back- reference does not introduce a target.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/AddManaEffectRule.cs)

### `BecomeMonarchEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target player becomes the monarch." — Rule 716 monarch designation granted to a chosen player.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/BecomeMonarchEffectRule.cs)

### `BecomesCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This [permanent] becomes a [P/T] [colors] [subtype] [card types] creature with [keyword] until end of turn." — the Keyrune/Monument "animate" template. Emits a single `BecomesCreatureEffect` describing the full set of characteristics the source permanent takes on for the duration.

There is no keyword <em>action</em> for "becomes a creature"; this is one continuous effect (CR 611) that sets card types/subtypes (CR 205), power/ toughness (CR 208), colors (CR 105), and grants a keyword ability (CR 113.6). Layer/timestamp ordering (CR 613) is engine territory and is not modeled.

Examples:

Azorius Keyrune — "This artifact becomes a 2/2 white and blue Bird artifact creature with flying until end of turn." Gruul Keyrune — "This artifact becomes a 3/2 red and green Beast artifact creature with trample until end of turn."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/BecomesCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `CantBeBlockedThisTurnEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This creature can't be blocked this turn." — activated ability effect that grants the controlling creature full unblockability until end of turn. CR 509.1b: "The defending player checks each creature they control to see whether it's affected by any restrictions (effects that say a creature can't block, or that it can't block unless some condition is met)." A "can't be blocked" effect is a blocking restriction on the attacker. Maps to `CantBeBlockedEffect` with:

`Target` = `Self` — the creature that pays the cost is the one that becomes unblockable. `Duration` = `untilEndOfTurn` — oracle phrase "this turn" maps to end-of-turn expiry. No `BlockedByFilter` — the restriction is unconditional.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CantBeBlockedThisTurnEffectRule.cs)

### `ChangeColorEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target creature becomes [color] until end of turn." — layer-5 color-changing continuous effect (CR 105.3). Recognizes the standard activated-ability pattern on a target creature.

CR 105.3 (verbatim): "Effects may change an object's color or give a color to a colorless object. If an effect gives an object a new color, the new color replaces all previous colors the object had (unless the effect said the object became that color 'in addition' to its other colors)."

Supported colors: white, blue, black, red, green, colorless (CR 105.1). Color codes follow the WUBRG convention used throughout the MAST schema.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ChangeColorEffectRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Create a [P]/[T] [color] [subtype] creature token." and the predefined artifact-token form "Create [count] [Treasure|Food|Clue|Blood] token(s)." Token creation as an activated-ability effect (Rule 111).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/CreateTokenEffectRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Destroy target &lt;type&gt;." — single-target destroy as an activated-ability effect. Handles bare card-type targets: creature, artifact, enchantment, land, planeswalker, permanent; and two-type disjunctions: "artifact or enchantment", "creature or planeswalker", etc. CR 701.8a: "To destroy a permanent, move it from the battlefield to its owner's graveyard." GUARD: handles only "Destroy target &lt;type&gt;" shapes. Does NOT handle Exile (sibling family F14 owns "Exile target creature").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DestroyTargetEffectRule.cs)

### `DiscardCardsEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Discard N cards" — "Discard up to two cards", "Discard a legendary card".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DiscardCardsEffectRule.cs)

### `DiscardCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Discard cost: "Discard a card", "Discard a legendary card", etc. Reuses the shared `ParseDiscardPattern`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DiscardCostRule.cs)

### `DrawCardsEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Draw N cards" — "Draw two cards", "Draw a card", "Each other player draws a card". "Each other player" maps to EachOtherPlayer (broader than EachOpponent in multiplayer, Rule 109.1 / 102.1).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/DrawCardsEffectRule.cs)

### `ExileFromGraveyardEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Exile target card from a graveyard." — single-card graveyard-exile as an activated-ability effect (Rule 701.7), the common graveyard-hoser pattern.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileFromGraveyardEffectRule.cs)

### `ExileSelfCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Exile-self cost: "Exile this artifact", "Exile this creature", etc. The activated permanent is moved to the exile zone as part of paying the activation cost. CR 701.13a: "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is." The encoded cost uses `ExileCost` with: - `Filter.CardTypes` = the permanent type named in oracle text - `Filter.Characteristics` = `[OtherCharacteristic("this permanent")]` to record the self-referential "this" phrasing (parallel to the `SacrificeCostRule` convention for "Sacrifice this artifact" costs) - `FromZone` = `Battlefield` (the permanent is on the battlefield when the ability is activated; CR 701.13a moves it from there to exile) - `Quantity` = 1 (oracle text always names a single permanent)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileSelfCostRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Exile target creature." — moves a targeted creature from whatever zone it occupies to the exile zone. Also handles the Aura/Equipment form: "Exile enchanted creature." — exiles the permanent this card is attached to (CR 303.4m). Not a targeted ability; no "target" keyword in oracle text. CR 701.13a: "To exile an object, move it to the exile zone from wherever it is." CR 303.4m: "An ability of a permanent that refers to the 'enchanted [object or player]' refers to whatever object or player that permanent is attached to…"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ExileTargetCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `GainAbilityEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"gains [keyword]" grants on a target. Supported shapes: - "This [type] gains [keyword] [until end of turn]" (Self) - "Target creature gains [keyword] until end of turn" - "Creatures you control gain [keyword]" (Each, creatures you control)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/GainAbilityEffectRule.cs)

### `GainLifeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"You gain N life" — N is a literal number, number word, or variable (X/Y/Z). "You gain X life.", "You gain 2 life.", "You gain three life."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/GainLifeEffectRule.cs)

### `GetsPTAndGainsKeywordEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This creature gets &lt;±P/±T&gt; and gains &lt;kw&gt; until end of turn" — a single-sentence TWO-clause activated effect that neither `ModifyPTEffectRule` nor `GainAbilityEffectRule` handles alone.

<b>CR 613.4c</b> (P/T modifier clause): "Layer 7c: Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness (but don't set power and/or toughness to a specific number or value) are applied." The granted keyword is a continuous ability grant that applies in layer 6 (ability-adding effects). Both clauses are present in oracle text; this parser describes them without encoding layer machinery.

Returns a `CompositeEffect` whose child list carries, in order:

A `ModifyPTEffect` targeting Self with EOT duration. A `GainAbilityEffect` targeting Self, the keyword ability, and EOT duration.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/GetsPTAndGainsKeywordEffectRule.cs)

### `InvestigateEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Investigate." — CR 701.16a: "Investigate" means "Create a Clue token." See rule 111.10f. Maps the bare keyword action to `InvestigateEffect` with no count (defaults to one Clue token). Reminder text has already been stripped by the activated-ability parser before this rule runs (Rule 207.2).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/InvestigateEffectRule.cs)

### `LoseLifeEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target player loses N life." — targeted life-loss as an activated-ability effect (Rule 119), covering drain artifacts like Onyx Goblet.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/LoseLifeEffectRule.cs)

### `ManaCostComponentRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Mana / tap / untap cost component: "{1}", "{2}{G}", "{T}", "{Q}". Returns a `TapCost` for {T}, an `UntapCost` for {Q}, otherwise a `ManaCost`. Only fires for components containing a mana brace.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ManaCostComponentRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Target creature gets [+-](N|X)/[+-](M|X) (until end of turn | for as long as [condition])." and the self-referential "This creature gets …" variant. Handles literal and variable modifiers; variable negation is a CalculatedQuantity with Operation = "negate" (see `ParseSignedModifier`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ModifyPTEffectRule.cs)

### `MonstrosityEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Monstrosity N" — maps the keyword-action to a `MonstrosityEffect`.

CR 701.37a: "Monstrosity N means 'If this permanent isn't monstrous, put N +1/+1 counters on it and it becomes monstrous.'" CR 701.37b: "Monstrous is a designation that has no rules meaning other than to act as a marker that the monstrosity action and other spells and abilities can identify."

The parenthetical reminder text "(If this creature isn't monstrous, put N +1/+1 counters on it and it becomes monstrous.)" is stripped upstream by `ActivatedAbilityParser.StripTrailingReminder` before this rule fires.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/MonstrosityEffectRule.cs)

### `PayLifeCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Pay-life cost: "Pay 1 life", "Pay 3 life" (Rule 118). The life payment is a cost that reduces the player's life total.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PayLifeCostRule.cs)

### `PreventNextDamageFromColorSourceEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"The next time a [color] source of your choice would deal damage to you this turn, prevent that damage." — the Circle/Rune of Protection one-shot prevention shield (e.g. Circle of Protection: Red, Rune of Protection: Red). Maps to `PreventDamageEffect` with `NextInstance` set (the "next instance, regardless of amount" semantics — distinct from a bounded `Amount` or the all-instances `All`), the damage `Source` as a `Designated` reference filtered by the named color ("a red source of your choice" — the controller designates which qualifying source at resolution; not a target, no targeting keyword in the oracle, and not controller-bound), the `Target` (recipient) as `You`, and an `untilTime`/end-of-turn `Duration` for "this turn". "of your choice" is recorded as the player-designated reference itself, not its resolution (ADR 0004 reference-not-resolution). This is CR-615 prevention, NOT a replacement effect — no replacement ordering/layering is modelled. CR 615.8 (cited verbatim): "Some prevention effects generated by the resolution of a spell or ability refer to the next time a specific source would deal damage. These effects prevent the next instance of damage from that source, regardless of how much damage that is. Once an instance of damage from that source has been prevented, any subsequent instances of damage that would be dealt by that source are dealt normally." CR 615.2 (cited verbatim): "Many prevention effects apply to damage from a source. See rule 609.7."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PreventNextDamageFromColorSourceEffectRule.cs)

### `PreventNextNDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Prevent the next N damage that would be dealt to any target this turn." Maps to `PreventDamageEffect` with a literal `Amount`, `AnyTarget` as the `Target`, and an `untilEndOfTurn` `Duration`. CR 615.8 (cited verbatim): "Some prevention effects generated by the resolution of a spell or ability refer to the next time a specific source would deal damage. These effects prevent the next instance of damage from that source, regardless of how much damage that is."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PreventNextNDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule.cs)

### `PutCountersEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Put N +1/+1 (or -1/-1) counters on [target]" — "Put a +1/+1 counter on this creature", "Put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/PutCountersEffectRule.cs)

### `RegenerateEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Regenerate this creature." / "Regenerate target [type]." (Rule 701.19). MAST records the effect and target only; shield / destruction-replacement semantics are engine territory.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/RegenerateEffectRule.cs)

### `RemoveCountersCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Remove-counters cost: "Remove three spore counters from this creature" / "Remove two charge counters from this permanent" (Rule 122). The counter type is a named counter; the source is `Self()` since oracle text always reads "from this creature / permanent".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/RemoveCountersCostRule.cs)

### `ReturnFromGraveyardToHandEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return this card from your graveyard to your hand." — the self-retrieval pattern on activated abilities (e.g. Abzan Devotee). Models a graveyard-to-hand zone change where the source zone is the controller's graveyard (CR 404) and the destination is the controller's hand (CR 402). The permanent is in the graveyard at the time the cost is paid and ability resolves; Target = Self with a Graveyard zone qualifier captures both the identity of the object and its source zone. Anchored on "from your graveyard" to avoid matching battlefield-self bounce (F6 sibling: "to its owner's hand"), which has no graveyard source qualifier.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnFromGraveyardToHandEffectRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return this {creature|permanent|Aura} to its owner's hand." — battlefield-self bounce (CR 402, hand zone). Matches the self-referential phrasing found on creatures like Darting Merfolk and Auras like Mourning/Conviction. Distinct from `ReturnToBattlefieldEffectRule` (which handles graveyard-source reanimation) and the sibling F5 rule (which handles "from your graveyard" sourced returns). CR 402: Return to hand is a plain zone change to the Hand zone; no dedicated keyword action exists for it. CR 303.4(m): "An ability of a permanent that refers to the 'enchanted [object or player]' refers to whatever object or player that permanent is attached to, even if the permanent with the ability isn't an Aura." — the self-subject here is the Aura itself ("this Aura"), not the enchanted object, so Self is correct. Anchor: "return this &lt;noun&gt; to its owner's hand" where &lt;noun&gt; is a self-referential permanent-type word (creature, permanent, Aura, enchantment, artifact, land).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnSelfToHandEffectRule.cs)

### `ReturnToBattlefieldEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Return target [X] from [zone] to the battlefield." — e.g. "Return target creature or Vehicle card from your graveyard to the battlefield." The full type-phrase is captured as a Characteristics entry and the source zone on Zone.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ReturnToBattlefieldEffectRule.cs)

### `SacrificeCostRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedCostRule

Sacrifice cost: "Sacrifice another creature", "Sacrifice X Squirrels", etc. Reuses the shared `ParseSacrificePattern`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SacrificeCostRule.cs)

### `ScryEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Scry N" — e.g. "Scry 2", "Scry 1" (Rule 701.18).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/ScryEffectRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToBattlefieldEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Search your library for a &lt;filter&gt; permanent card with mana value N or less, put it onto the battlefield[tapped], then shuffle." Covers the activated tutor-to-battlefield pattern (e.g. Amrou Scout, Lin Sivvi, Deceptive Landscape, Contaminated Landscape). CR 701.23a: "To search for a card in a zone, look at all cards in that zone (even if it's a hidden zone) and find a card that matches the given description." Maps to a CompositeEffect containing: 1. SearchLibraryEffect (Destination = Battlefield or BattlefieldTapped) — the search and placement. 2. ShuffleEffect (Player = You) — the mandatory library shuffle. The ObjectFilter carries: - Subtypes: [subtype] when the qualifier is a creature-type (e.g. "Rebel") - Subtypes: [LandType1, LandType2, ...] when a basic-land subtype disjunction is present (e.g. "basic Plains, Swamp, or Forest" — CR 305.6: "The basic land types are Plains, Island, Swamp, Mountain, and Forest.") - CardTypes: ["permanent"] when a card-type constraint is present - ManaValueComparison: LessThanOrEqual N when "with mana value N or less"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SearchLibraryToBattlefieldEffectRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

Two shapes — both map to self-as-source dealDamage:

"[Self] deals N damage to any target." → AnyTarget (CR 119.2: damage is dealt by the source; "any target" selects a creature, player, or planeswalker). "[Self] deals N damage to target creature." → Target with creature filter (CR 119.2: narrower form restricted to creatures only).

GUARD: does NOT match "each opponent" (family F17) — that shape has no "target" keyword.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"[This creature|CardName] deals N damage to target attacking or blocking creature." — archer / ballista tap-to-deal-damage pattern. Both subject forms encode the source as `Self()`; the target is a creature with Characteristics = [Characteristic.Other("attacking or blocking")].

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureEffectRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToEachOpponentEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"This creature deals N damage to each opponent." — self-as-source dealDamage spreading N damage to every opponent simultaneously (CR 119.2: "Damage dealt to a player normally causes that player to lose that much life."). Guard: anchors on "each opponent"; does NOT match "any target" (handled by `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetEffectRule`) or "target attacking or blocking creature" (`SelfDealsDamageToAttackingOrBlockingCreatureEffectRule`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToEachOpponentEffectRule.cs)

### `SurveilEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Surveil N" as an activated-ability effect — e.g. "{4}, {T}: Surveil 1." CR 701.25a: "To 'surveil N' means to look at the top N cards of your library, then put any number of them into your graveyard and the rest on top of your library in any order."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/SurveilEffectRule.cs)

### `TapEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Tap [count] target [type]" — "Tap target creature", "Tap X target lands", "Tap two target creatures" (Rule 701.21). For variable-X abilities the X in cost and effect refer to the same chosen value (Rule 107.3b/c).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/TapEffectRule.cs)

### `TransformSelfEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Transform this creature" / "Transform this permanent" — the activated transform-self ability on the front face of a double-faced card (e.g. "{6}: Transform this creature.").

CR 701.27a: "To transform a permanent, turn it over so that its other face is up. Only permanents represented by double-faced tokens and double-faced cards can transform. (See rule 712, 'Double-Faced Cards.')"

Maps to a `TransformEffect` with a `Self` subject. The activation cost (including Phyrexian hybrid mana such as {B/P} — CR 107.4f) is parsed upstream by the cost-component rules, the "Activate only as a sorcery" restriction is lifted by `ActivatedAbilityParser.ExtractActivationRestrictions`, and the parenthetical reminder explaining Phyrexian mana is stripped by `ActivatedAbilityParser.StripTrailingReminder` before this rule fires.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/TransformSelfEffectRule.cs)

### `UntapEffectRule` — *sealed class* : IActivatedEffectRule

"Untap target [subtype]" — "Untap target Forest.", "Untap target creature.", "Untap another target creature or land [you control]." First-cut single-token target recognition (Rule 701.20). Also handles self-reference form: "Untap this creature.", "Untap this artifact.", "Untap this permanent." — per CR 701.26b: "To untap a permanent, rotate it back to the upright position from a sideways position. Only tapped permanents can be untapped."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Activated/Rules/UntapEffectRule.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Spell/`

### `IMultiSpellRule` — *interface*

Extension of `ISpellRule` for shapes that expand to a flat list of effects on `Effects` rather than a single `Effect`. Implement this alongside `ISpellRule` (returning `false` from `TryMatch` so the single-effect path never fires) when the oracle-text shape naturally produces multiple sibling effects that should be represented as a flat array, not a `CompositeEffect`.

Discovery: `SpellAbilityParser` picks this up automatically for any `SpellRuleAttribute`-decorated class that also implements this interface.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/IMultiSpellRule.cs)

### `ISpellRule` — *interface*

One spell-effect recognition rule. Each implementation maps a single oracle-text shape to its `Effect` AST node. Rules are discovered by reflection at `SpellAbilityParser` construction via the `SpellRuleAttribute` decoration and dispatched in descending `Priority` order — first match wins.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/ISpellRule.cs)

### `SpellRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `ISpellRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `SpellAbilityParser`. Each decorated rule contributes one oracle-text recognizer to the parser's effect cascade.

<b>Priority convention (range 0–100, default 50, higher = more specific = tried first):</b>

<b>80–100</b> — rules that supersede a more-general sibling (color-disjunction before type-disjunction, IfYouDo variants before plain variants, "this creature" before "a [type]"). Without the bump these would never fire because the more-general rule would shadow them. <b>50</b> — default. Non-overlapping rules whose regex anchors are mutually exclusive. <b>20–40</b> — catch-all / generic rules that should be tried last so a more-specific sibling gets a chance first.

The numeric scheme replaces the old source-order convention: each rule used to declare its priority implicitly by where it appeared in `TryParseEffect`'s cascade. Splitting rules into one-file-per-rule loses that ordering signal, so each rule now states its priority explicitly.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/SpellRuleAttribute.cs)

### `SpellRuleHelpers` — *static class*

Shared utilities used across multiple `ISpellRule` implementations. Lives outside the dispatcher so rules can be added without taking a dependency on `SpellAbilityParser` internals.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/SpellRuleHelpers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/`

### `AddManaSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Add {mana}." — bare add-mana spell instruction (e.g., Infernal Plunge's main effect). Covers the same oracle pattern that ActivatedAbilityParser handles on the right-hand side of a mana ability cost, but here it appears as the whole spell body after the additional-cost prefix has been stripped by ClauseSplitter.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/AddManaSpellRule.cs)

### `AllMustBlockTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"All creatures able to block target creature this turn do so." — Lure-type spell. Rule 509.1c forcing requirement applied transiently until end of turn.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/AllMustBlockTargetRule.cs)

### `BiteRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Recognises "bite" spell oracle text — a one-directional fight where only the controlled creature deals damage:

"Target creature you control deals damage equal to its power to target creature you don't control." "Target creature you control deals damage equal to its power to target creature or planeswalker you don't control."

Both phrasings emit a `DealDamageEffect` where:

Source is the controlled target creature (Controller: You). Amount is `DerivedQuantity` Power sourced from "it". Target is an opponent-controlled creature (or creature/planeswalker for the broader form).

Distinct from `FightRule` (CR 701.14), which produces a symmetric `FightEffect` — both creatures deal damage to each other. Bite is asymmetric: only the source creature deals damage; the target does not retaliate.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/BiteRule.cs)

### `CantBeCounteredRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"This spell can't be countered." — encoded as a spell-level effect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CantBeCounteredRule.cs)

### `CastSpellThisTurnDelayedDrawRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Whenever you cast a[n] [type] spell this turn, draw a card." — a spell whose resolution creates a delayed triggered ability (CR 603.7) bounded to this turn: the trigger is an event (a spell of the given type is cast by you), the window is "this turn" (`untilTime` end of turn), the effect is a draw. ADR 0002/0004. Examples: Glimpse of Nature (creature), Beck.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CastSpellThisTurnDelayedDrawRule.cs)

### `ChangeTargetRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Change the target of target spell with a single target." Rule 115.7 — the controller of this spell or ability may choose a new legal target for the targeted spell. Covers Divert, Misdirection, and functional equivalents.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/ChangeTargetRule.cs)

### `CounterAbilityRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

Counters that target activated or triggered abilities (or both), or the full "spell, activated ability, or triggered ability" disjunction. Rule 701.6. Handles these oracle-text shapes:

"Counter target activated ability." "Counter target triggered ability." "Counter target activated or triggered ability." (Stifle) "Counter target spell, activated ability, or triggered ability." (Disallow)

The target filter uses `CardTypes` to record which stack-object categories are in scope: `"activatedAbility"`, `"triggeredAbility"`, and/or `"spell"` — parallel to the way `CounterSpellRule` uses `CardTypes: ["spell"]` for plain spell targets. Priority 80: more specific than `CounterSpellRule` (priority 50) and `CounterTargetTypeOrSubtypeSpellRule` (priority 80 by name-match conflict resolved by the "activated/triggered" literal tokens not being colour words, so no actual overlap there — but the explicit bump keeps ordering stable).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CounterAbilityRule.cs)

### `CounterSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Counter target spell." with optional color/type qualifiers and an optional "unless its controller pays {cost}" tail. Rule 701.6. Handles these filter dimensions in one consolidated surface: - color words (white, blue, black, red, green, colorless, multicolored) - non&lt;color&gt; predicates (nonblue, nonred, …) → Characteristics - bare card-type qualifier before "spell" (artifact, land, enchantment, …) → CardTypes - "with mana value N" → ManaValueComparison - "unless its controller pays {cost}" where cost is any mana expression ({1}, {X}, {1}{U}, etc.) — parsed via ManaCostParser

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Spell/Rules/CounterSpellRule.cs)

### `CounterTargetTypeOrSubtypeSpellRule` — *sealed class* : ISpellRule

"Counter target [Q1] or [Q2] spell." — Nullify ("creature or Aura"), Hisoka's Defiance ("Spirit or Arcane"). Token type classified by vocabulary, not position. Priority 80: overrides `CounterSpellRule` whose color-disjunction branch could otherwise capture two-token disjunctions.
