### `ReconfigureStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Reconfigure [cost]" oracle line into two `ActivatedAbility` nodes, one per ability defined by CR 702.151a.

CR 702.151a (verbatim): "Reconfigure represents two activated abilities. Reconfigure [cost] means "[Cost]: Attach this permanent to another target creature you control. Activate only as a sorcery" and "[Cost]: Unattach this permanent. Activate only if this permanent is attached to a creature and only as a sorcery."

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The activation condition "only if this permanent is attached to a creature" is a runtime-state guard with no existing structured node; it is omitted per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ReconfigureStaticRule.cs)

### `ReplicateStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Replicate [cost]" oracle line into the two abilities defined by CR 702.56a (mirroring the `ReconfigureStaticRule` / `DashStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.56a (verbatim): "Replicate is a keyword that represents two abilities... 'Replicate [cost]' means 'As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may pay [cost] any number of times' and 'When you cast this spell, if a replicate cost was paid for it, copy it for each time its replicate cost was paid. If the spell has any targets, you may choose new targets for any of the copies.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `ReplicateKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary additional-cost static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Replicate decomposes into two).

"you may choose new targets for any of the copies" is engine territory (target reselection) and is omitted per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/ReplicateStaticRule.cs)

### `RevealOrEntersTappedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Reveal-or-enters-tapped land ("Snarl" cycle, Strixhaven): "As this land enters, you may reveal a [TypeA] or [TypeB] card from your hand. If you don't, this land enters tapped."

Modelled descriptively as the checkland/fastland shape — the composite "as this enters, tap it" (`When = StaticTimingKind.AsThisEnters` on the `StaticAbility`, a plain `TapEffect` targeting self) gated by `Condition`. The condition names the predicate under which the land enters UNtapped (the controller revealing a qualifying card): the land enters tapped whenever that does not happen. The "you may reveal" choice is recorded as the condition's text rather than as a game action — MAST describes the card, it does not execute the reveal (descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

The reveal is a pure entry gate carrying no quantified resource, so it collapses cleanly onto the `Condition` axis. This differs from the painland/shockland template (`PainlandRule`) where the optional action is a life payment — a quantified cost that warrants its own `PayLifeEffect`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/RevealOrEntersTappedRule.cs)

### `SelfPTForEachAuraAttachedRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

"This creature gets +N/+N for each Aura attached to it." — the Kor Spiritdancer family. Distinct from `SelfPTForEachRule` in two ways: (1) the counted set is "Aura attached to it" (an attachment relationship, not a "you control" board count), and (2) the per-Aura increment may exceed 1 (Spiritdancer is +2/+2 per Aura), which `SelfPTForEachRule` explicitly defers to a "distinct family shape" by returning null on multiplier &gt; 1.

The dynamic amount is "N × (number of Auras attached)". A per-each increment of 1 reuses a bare `CountQuantity` (matching the single-increment convention); an increment &gt; 1 wraps that count in a `CalculatedQuantity` with `Operation = "multiply"`, mirroring the established CalculatedQuantity-wrapping convention (e.g. the "negate" wrapper in `ActivatedRuleHelpers.ParseSignedModifier`). No new `Quantity` subtype is introduced — both discriminators (`count`, `calculated`) already exist.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SelfPTForEachAuraAttachedRule.cs)

### `SelfPTForEachRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SelfPTForEachRule.cs)

### `SkipUntapRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SkipUntapRule.cs)

### `SpellsTargetingCostMoreRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SpellsTargetingCostMoreRule.cs)

### `SquadStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a "Squad [cost]" oracle line into the two linked abilities defined by CR 702.157a (mirroring the `DashStaticRule` / `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.157a (verbatim): "Squad is a keyword that represents two linked abilities... 'Squad [cost]' means 'As an additional cost to cast this spell, you may pay [cost] any number of times' and 'When this creature enters, if its squad cost was paid, create a token that's a copy of it for each time its squad cost was paid.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `SquadKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary additional-cost static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Squad decomposes into two).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/SquadStaticRule.cs)

### `TokenAugmentationReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/TokenAugmentationReplacementRule.cs)

### `TokenDoublingReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/TokenDoublingReplacementRule.cs)

### `TokenSubstitutionReplacementRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/TokenSubstitutionReplacementRule.cs)

### `TribalAnthemModifyPTRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/TribalAnthemModifyPTRule.cs)

### `TypeSpellCostReductionRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/TypeSpellCostReductionRule.cs)

### `UnleashStaticRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Decomposes a bare "Unleash" oracle line into the two static abilities defined by CR 702.98a (mirroring the `DashStaticRule` / `ReconfigureStaticRule` multi-ability precedent).

CR 702.98a (verbatim): "Unleash is a keyword that represents two static abilities. 'Unleash' means 'You may have this permanent enter with an additional +1/+1 counter on it' and 'This permanent can't block as long as it has a +1/+1 counter on it.'"

Priority 1001 — fires before `KeywordListRule` (priority 1000) so the two-ability decomposition takes precedence over the single-ability keyword combinator path. The combinator in `UnleashKeyword` remains live as a fallback, emitting only the primary enters-with-counter static ability (the keyword expander returns a single `Ability`; Unleash decomposes into two).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/UnleashStaticRule.cs)

### `WardKeywordRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/WardKeywordRule.cs)

### `WithKeywordAnthemModifyPTRule` — *sealed class* : IStaticRule

Recognises the "Other creatures you control with [keyword] get +X/+Y" anthem shape. A continuous P/T modification (CR 613.1, layer 7c) scoped to permanents the controller owns that carry a particular keyword. The keyword predicate is a "has [keyword]" filter encoded on `Characteristics` (e.g. `"flying"`), consistent with how game-state predicates like "tapped" and "with a +1/+1 counter" are encoded by sibling rules. Example oracle text: "Other creatures you control with flying get +1/+0." → ObjectFilter { CardTypes: ["creature"], Controller: You, Characteristics: ["other", "flying"] }

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Static/Rules/WithKeywordAnthemModifyPTRule.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/`

### `ITriggerConditionRule` — *interface*

One trigger-condition recognition rule (the event + filter half of a triggered ability). Implementations are discovered by reflection via `TriggerConditionRuleAttribute` and dispatched by `TriggeredAbilityParser` in descending `Priority` order, then ordinal name for tie-breaking; the first non-null result wins.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/ITriggerConditionRule.cs)

### `ITriggeredRule` — *interface*

One triggered-effect recognition rule. Dispatched by `TriggeredAbilityParser` in priority order (descending). The `text` input is the post-trigger effect fragment, already split off by the trigger/effect boundary detection in the dispatcher.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/ITriggeredRule.cs)

### `TriggerConditionRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `ITriggerConditionRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `TriggeredAbilityParser`. Mirrors `TriggeredRuleAttribute` for the trigger-condition side.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/TriggerConditionRuleAttribute.cs)

### `TriggeredRuleAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute, MagicAST.Parsing.Parsers.IPrioritizedRuleAttribute

Marks an `ITriggeredRule` implementation for reflection-based discovery by `TriggeredAbilityParser`. See `SpellRuleAttribute` for the shared priority convention (0–100, default 50, higher = more specific = tried first).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/TriggeredRuleAttribute.cs)

### `TriggeredRuleHelpers` — *static class*

Shared utilities used across `ITriggeredRule` implementations.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/TriggeredRuleHelpers.cs)

## `/Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/`

### `AddManaRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"add {...}" / "add one mana of any color" — Rule 106. Note: triggered mana production is NOT a Rule 605 mana ability, so no IsManaAbility flag is set.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AddManaRule.cs)

### `AnotherSubtypeEntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"another [Subtype] you control enters" — subtype-filtered enters trigger. Handles the Ally-rally pattern and analogous mechanics where the trigger fires when another permanent of a specific creature subtype enters the battlefield under your control. Rule 603.1: triggered abilities use "when," "whenever," or "at" to watch for zone changes; the subtype filter narrows which entering permanents cause the ability to trigger. Examples: "Whenever another Ally you control enters, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AnotherSubtypeEntersConditionRule.cs)

### `AttachTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"attach it to target [filter] you control" — an explicit attach instruction inside a triggered ability. Covers the ETB auto-attach pattern common on Equipment: "When this Equipment enters, attach it to target creature you control."

Rule 701.3: "To take an Aura, Equipment, or Fortification from where it currently is and put it onto a specified object or player." MAST records the oracle-text instruction descriptively; the legality checks, zone-change details, and resulting continuous effects are engine territory.

Distinct from `EquipEffect`, which models the Equip activated-ability keyword (Rule 702.6). This rule handles explicit "attach it to target" instructions in triggered ability effects.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttachTriggeredRule.cs)

### `AttackWithAndAnotherConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you attack with [Name] and another [qualifier] creature" (Merry shape) — models the companion-attack trigger (Rule 508). Emits `Attacks` with a companion-creature filter carrying the "another" characteristic plus any supertype qualifier.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttackWithAndAnotherConditionRule.cs)

### `AttacksConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [CardName] attacks" / "Whenever this creature attacks" / "Whenever a creature you control attacks" — emits `Attacks` (Rule 508 — Declare Attackers). The filter shape is shared with dies/enters via `ParseObjectFilter`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttacksConditionRule.cs)

### `AttacksOrBlocksConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] attacks or blocks" — combined combat trigger (Rule 508/509), modelled as a single `AttacksOrBlocks` event. Tried before the individual Attacks/Blocks rules (higher priority) so the disjunction isn't partially matched.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AttacksOrBlocksConditionRule.cs)

### `AuraDealsDamageToPlayerOrPlaneswalkerRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"this Aura deals N damage to that player or a planeswalker that player controls." — the upkeep-damage trigger of a player-enchanting Curse (Curse of the Pierced Heart). The "or" is a single chooser-selected reference, so the damage Target is a `Choice` over two options: the enchanted player ("that player", on a player Aura = the enchanted player) and a planeswalker that player controls.

Rule 120 (dealing damage); Rule 702.5 (Enchant player — the enchanted player is the Aura's enchanted object). The chooser picks exactly one option at resolution, so a single Choice reference is correct rather than two targets.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/AuraDealsDamageToPlayerOrPlaneswalkerRule.cs)

### `BecomeMonarchTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you become the monarch." — Rule 725. The controller of the source permanent becomes the monarch game-state designation. MAST records the descriptive instruction; the draw-at-end-step and combat-damage-transfer rules (725.2–725.6) are engine territory.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BecomeMonarchTriggeredRule.cs)

### `BecomesBlockedConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this creature becomes blocked" — Rule 509 (Declare Blockers Step). Fires on the attacking creature when one or more blockers are assigned to it. The `BecomesBlocked` event is distinct from `Blocks` (the blocking creature's trigger).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BecomesBlockedConditionRule.cs)

### `BecomesCrewedConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this Vehicle becomes crewed [for the first time each turn]" — Rule 702.122 trigger.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BecomesCrewedConditionRule.cs)

### `BecomesTargetConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When this creature becomes the target of a spell or ability" — triggered-ability machinery: Rule 603.1-603.2; "becomes the target" relationship: Rule 115.1. The subject "this creature" is the source permanent; only the self-reference shape is modelled here.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BecomesTargetConditionRule.cs)

### `BlocksConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this creature blocks" / "Whenever [CardName] blocks" / "Whenever this creature blocks a creature" — Rule 509 (Declare Blockers Step). Excludes "becomes blocked" (BecomesBlocked event), which also contains "blocks".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BlocksConditionRule.cs)

### `BlocksOrBecomesBlockedConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this creature blocks or becomes blocked" — combined combat trigger (CR 702.45 Bushido). Fires from the perspective of BOTH the blocking creature (Rule 509 — it declared as a blocker) AND the attacked creature (Rule 509 — a blocker was assigned to it). Modelled as a single `BlocksOrBecomesBlocked` event. Tried before the individual Blocks/BecomesBlocked rules (higher priority) so the disjunction is not partially matched.

CR 702.45 (verbatim): "Bushido is a triggered ability. 'Bushido N' means 'Whenever this creature blocks or becomes blocked, it gets +N/+N until end of turn.'"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/BlocksOrBecomesBlockedConditionRule.cs)

### `CantBlockThisTurnTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Handles the triggered-ability effect shape "target [filter] can't block this turn." CR 603.6a: Enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield. Written "When [this object] enters, ..." or "Whenever a [type] enters, ..." CR 509.1 (declare-blockers step): a "can't block" restriction applies during the declare-blockers turn-based action. The effect is durative — it expires at the end of the current turn (`untilEndOfTurn`).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CantBlockThisTurnTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CastAuraSpellConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you cast an Aura spell" (Kor Spiritdancer family). Aura is an enchantment <em>subtype</em> (Rule 205.3h), so the cast filter is recorded as `Subtypes = ["Aura"]` rather than a card-type characteristic — the distinction that `SpellCastConditionRule` cannot express (its per-word loop matches card types like "creature"/"noncreature", and it has no subtype channel). Sits above `SpellCastConditionRule` (priority 998) so the Aura subtype qualifier is captured instead of being silently dropped by the generic spell-cast recognizer.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CastAuraSpellConditionRule.cs)

### `ConditionalPayTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Handles "you may pay {COST}. If you do, [effect]." conditional-pay triggers and the discard-as-cost loot variant "you may discard a card. If you do, draw a card."

Two shapes:

<b>Mana-pay shape</b>: "you may pay {N}. If you do, [EFFECT]." → `ConditionalPayEffect` with the mana cost on `Cost` and the parsed consequent on `IfYouDo`. Example: "Whenever a creature dies, you may pay {1}. If you do, you gain 1 life." (Deathgreeter, ALA). <b>Discard-loot shape</b>: "you may discard [N] card(s). If you do, draw [M] card(s)." → `DiscardCardsEffect` with `IsOptional = true` and `IfYouDo = DrawCardsEffect`. Mirrors the spell-level `DiscardThenDrawSpellRule`.

Priority 80: both shapes contain the trigger text "you may [action]. If you do," as a prefix. Without elevated priority the discard-loot shape would race against the plain `EachOpponentDiscardsRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ConditionalPayTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ControlNoLandTypeConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When you control no [LandSubtype]" — state-triggered ability (Rule 603). Fires when the controlling player transitions to controlling no lands of the named basic-land subtype (e.g. "When you control no Islands, sacrifice this creature."). Expanded oracle for the obsolete Islandhome family. The land subtype is stored in Filter.Subtypes; CardTypes=["land"], Controller=You.

Supported subtypes: Island, Forest, Swamp, Mountain, Plains.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ControlNoLandTypeConditionRule.cs)

### `CreateCopyOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create a token that's a copy of this creature" — token-copy effect that fires as the resolution clause of a `DealsCombatDamageToPlayer` trigger.

Rule 707.2: A token that is a copy of an object copies the copiable values of that object. Rule 111.8: Such tokens are created on the battlefield under their creator's control. The copy source is the card itself ("this creature"), modelled as `Self`.

Priority 70 — more specific than the generic `CreateTokenRule` (50) so this rule is tried first and the generic rule never sees the "copy of this creature" phrasing.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateCopyOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule.cs)

### `CreateTokenRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"create [article] [P/T] [colors] [subtypes] creature token [with ...]" — Rule 111. Also handles predefined artifact tokens (Food, Treasure, Clue, Blood) which have no P/T and whose activated ability is reminder text only (Rule 107.10b). Handles the optional "you may" prefix (Rule 116.1b) by setting IsOptional = true on the resulting CreateTokenEffect.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/CreateTokenRule.cs)

### `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] deals combat damage to (a player|an opponent|any player)" — emits `DealsCombatDamageToPlayer` (Rule 510 — Combat Damage Step; Rule 603.6 — triggered abilities). The recipient class is implied by the enum value; the Filter captures the subject (what is dealing the damage).

"a creature you control with [keyword] deals combat damage to a player" — the "with [keyword]" suffix on the subject noun phrase is a keyword-ability characteristic constraint on the matched object (CR 702 — keyword abilities). Modelled as `Characteristics` carrying a structured `KeywordCharacteristic` so the filter is fully typed with no free-text residual.

CR 510.1 (Combat Damage Step): "First, the active player announces how each attacking creature assigns its combat damage … This turn-based action doesn't use the stack." CR 603.2: "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsCombatDamageConditionRule.cs)

### `DealsCombatDamageToCreatureConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] deals combat damage to a creature" — emits `DealsCombatDamageToCreature`.

CR 510.1 (Combat Damage Step): "First, the active player announces how each attacking creature assigns its combat damage, then the defending player announces how each blocking creature assigns its combat damage. This turn-based action doesn't use the stack." The combat-damage assignment is the game event; CR 603.2 — "Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers."

The recipient class (a creature) is implied by the enum value; the Filter captures the subject — the thing dealing the damage ("this creature", self-by-name, etc.). Sits above `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` (player recipient, Priority 985) and `DealsDamageConditionRule` (any damage, Priority 984) so the creature-recipient variant is recognised before the broader shapes; those sibling rules guard on "to a player" / exclude "deals combat damage" respectively, so there is no overlap.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsCombatDamageToCreatureConditionRule.cs)

### `DealsDamageConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever [subject] deals damage" — any damage (Rule 120), not only combat damage. Emits `DealsDamage`. Covers lifelink-analog oracle text printed before the lifelink keyword existed. The combat variant is handled by `DealsCombatDamageConditionRule` (higher priority), so this excludes it.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsDamageConditionRule.cs)

### `DealsThatMuchDamageToEachOtherOpponentRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals that much damage to each other opponent." — reflexive combat-damage spread (Grenzo's Ruffians pattern). Paired with a DealsCombatDamageToPlayer trigger; the "that much" refers to the amount of combat damage dealt to the trigger's original recipient, and "each other opponent" is every opponent other than that recipient. Rule 120.1: a source deals damage. Rule 510 (Combat Damage Step): the trigger fires when combat damage is dealt to an opponent. "Each other opponent" is the set of opponents excluding the one already hit; this is recorded descriptively as an EachOpponent reference with Characteristics: ["other"] — the "other" predicate's runtime resolution (relative to the trigger's opponent) is engine territory per the descriptive-not-engine doctrine. Rule 702.121 (Melee) lists Grenzo's Ruffians as a canonical example of this pattern. The rule encodes "that much" as a CalculatedQuantity with Expression="that much" and Operation="match", matching the convention established for Chatterfang's "that many" token-augmentation replacement.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DealsThatMuchDamageToEachOtherOpponentRule.cs)

### `DestroyTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"destroy target [filter]." / "you may destroy target [filter]." — single-target destroy on the triggered side. Delegates single-noun filter parsing to `ParseTargetFilter` (same lexical surface as `DestroyTargetSimpleRule`). Handles type-disjunction filters ("artifact or enchantment", "creature, artifact, or enchantment") via `SplitTypeDisjunction`, matching the spell-side `DestroyTargetTypeDisjunctionRule`. Covers: land, artifact, enchantment, creature, permanent, and richer filter phrases (color + type, non- prefix, etc.), as well as any two-or-more-type disjunction. The optional "you may" prefix sets `IsOptional` = <see langword="true"/>.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DestroyTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `DiesConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"dies" triggers: "this creature dies", "a creature dies", "another creature dies", etc. "dies" is modern oracle; the longform "is put into a graveyard from the battlefield" (Rule 700.4) describes the same event and normalises to the same TriggerEvent.Dies.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DiesConditionRule.cs)

### `DiscardOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"that player discards a card" — discard effect imposed on the player who received combat damage. Fires as the effect clause of a `DealsCombatDamageToPlayer` trigger (Rule 510 — Combat Damage Step). "That player" refers back to the player mentioned in the trigger condition (Rule 701.9 — Discard).

Covers the canonical single-card form. The N-card variant ("that player discards N cards") is also handled: count is parsed from a word-or-digit group so both shapes land on the same rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DiscardOnCombatDamageTriggeredRule.cs)

### `DrawCardConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you draw a card" — draw-card trigger (Rule 121: Drawing a Card). Fires whenever the controller draws a card by any means. Controller defaults to You.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DrawCardConditionRule.cs)

### `DrawCardsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"draw a card" with optional "unless that player pays {N}" tail.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DrawCardsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `DrawNthCardEachTurnConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you draw your second card each turn" — ordinal-qualified draw trigger (Rule 121: Drawing a Card). Unlike the unqualified `DrawCardConditionRule` ("you draw a card"), this shape names a specific occurrence within the turn ("your <i>second</i> card") and a per-turn counting window ("each turn"). MAST records those two qualifiers descriptively on the `TriggerCondition` (`Ordinal` and `PerTurn`) rather than encoding any turn-state counting machinery — Rule 603.2 makes the event-match the trigger; the ordinal merely narrows which match counts. Controller defaults to You.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/DrawNthCardEachTurnConditionRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentDiscardsRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"each opponent discards a card" — ETB discard effect imposed on all opponents simultaneously. Rule 701.9 (Discard). MAST records the discard event and the player scope (EachOpponent); the simultaneous discard order for multiplayer is engine territory, not described by the oracle text.

This rule covers the bare single-card form only. Counts other than one (e.g., "each opponent discards two cards") are a separate shape handled by extension once corpus pressure warrants.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EachOpponentDiscardsRule.cs)

### `EachOpponentLosesLifeTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"each opponent loses N life" — symmetric drain trigger (Marauding Blight-Priest pattern). Covers triggered effects where all opponents lose a fixed life amount simultaneously.

Rule 119.3: "If a player would lose life, that player's life total is reduced by that amount." Rule 102.2: "An opponent is each other player." In multiplayer, this effect applies to each opponent simultaneously; the simultaneous-resolution ordering is engine territory, not described by oracle text.

This rule handles the bare N-life form only. The compound drain pattern "each opponent loses N life and you gain N life" is handled by `TryParseEachOpponentLoseAndYouGainLife` in the multi-effect composite path.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EachOpponentLosesLifeTriggeredRule.cs)

### `EachPlayerDrawsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"each player draws a card" / "each opponent draws a card" — draw effect applied to all players simultaneously or to all opponents simultaneously. Rule 121 (Drawing a Card). MAST records the draw event and the player scope (EachPlayer or EachOpponent); the simultaneous draw order for multiplayer is engine territory, not described by the oracle text.

This rule covers the bare single-card form only. The composite "each player draws a card and loses N life" (Stormfist Crusader shape) is handled by the `TryParseEachPlayerDrawAndLoseLife` orchestrator in `TriggeredAbilityParser`, which runs before this rule and takes priority.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EachPlayerDrawsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `EntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"enters" triggers: "this creature enters", "a creature enters", etc.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EntersConditionRule.cs)

### `EntersOrDiesConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"enters or dies" compound triggers — fires on either zone-change event. Rule 603: "When [subject] enters or dies, [effect]." The oracle phrase "enters or dies" (always in that order) denotes a single triggered ability watching for the permanent either entering the battlefield or dying. Must be tried before the individual Enters/Dies rules (higher priority) so the disjunction isn't split and misclassified.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EntersOrDiesConditionRule.cs)

### `EtbTeamPumpTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"Creatures you control get +N/+M [and gain &lt;keyword&gt;] until end of turn." ETB team-pump pattern: the triggering creature buffs the whole team. The keyword grant is optional — both shapes are covered by one rule:

"Creatures you control get +1/+0 until end of turn." "Creatures you control get +1/+1 and gain haste until end of turn."

When only P/T is present the rule emits a bare `ModifyPTEffect`. When a keyword follows "and gain" the rule wraps both effects in a `CompositeEffect`, matching the conjunctive-modifier gold convention used on the static side (Rule 611 — continuous effects applied simultaneously). Recognised keywords (the set that appears in existing oracle corpus): haste, trample, flying, first strike, double strike, reach, lifelink, vigilance, deathtouch, menace, indestructible, hexproof, shroud.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/EtbTeamPumpTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExileTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"exile target [filter]." — single-target permanent exile on the triggered side. Delegates filter parsing to `ParseTargetFilter` (same lexical surface as `ExileTargetSimpleRule`). Covers bare card-type targets (creature, permanent, artifact, enchantment, planeswalker) and richer filter phrases via `ParseTargetFilter`:

Subtype-only: "Spirit", "Human" Color + card type: "black creature", "white artifact" non- prefix + card type: "nonblack creature", "nontoken creature"

This rule handles PERMANENT exile (no "until [this] leaves the battlefield" suffix). The `ExileUntilLeavesTriggeredRule` handles the temporary Oblivion Ring shape. Rule 701.13 (exile action) + Rule 205.3 (card types).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExileTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExileUntilLeavesTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"exile target nonland permanent an opponent controls until this [type] leaves the battlefield." — the Oblivion Ring / Hieromancer's Cage ETB exile shape. The effect is a temporary exile: the permanent returns when this [type] leaves the battlefield (Rule 611 — continuous effects with duration). MAST records the exile action descriptively (Rule 701.13 — Exile) with an `UntilLeavesBattlefieldDuration` whose `Object` is the literal self-reference phrase from oracle text ("this enchantment", "this creature", etc.). The LTB return ability is engine territory (Rule 603.7d — linked triggered abilities); MAST does not model it (descriptive-not-engine doctrine).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExileUntilLeavesTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ExploreTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it explores." — Rule 701.44a keyword action on the triggered side. Matches the canonical ETB-explore pattern where the entering creature (referred to as "it") performs the Explore keyword action.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ExploreTriggeredRule.cs)

### `GainControlTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"gain control of target [filter] [until end of turn]" — single-target control-change on the triggered side (ETB, dies, upkeep, etc.). Delegates single-noun filter parsing to `ParseTargetFilter`. Also handles the controller qualifier "an opponent controls" after the filter phrase, which is the canonical way oracle text restricts steal effects to opposing permanents (Rule 109.5 — control relationship). Duration is optional:

"until end of turn" → `UntilEndOfTurnDuration` No duration clause → <see langword="null"/> (permanent control change)

The optional "you may" prefix sets `IsOptional` = <see langword="true"/>. Recognized filter nouns (via `ParseTargetFilter`): creature, permanent, artifact, enchantment, planeswalker, land, and richer filter phrases (color + type, non- prefix, etc.). Rule 701.3 (control-changing effects) + Rule 115.1 (target).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/GainControlTriggeredRule.cs)

### `GainEnergyTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you get {E}", "you get {E}{E}", ... — energy-counter gain triggered effect. Rule 107.14: Energy counters are a player resource represented by the {E} symbol. Each "{E}" symbol in the oracle text represents one energy counter; we count the symbols to derive the literal amount.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/GainEnergyTriggeredRule.cs)

### `GainsLifeConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you gain life" — life-gain trigger. Controller defaults to You, flips to Opponent when the oracle text names an opponent.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/GainsLifeConditionRule.cs)

### `HauntCompoundConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When this creature enters or the creature it haunts dies" — Haunt compound trigger (Rule 702.55). Fires in two situations: (1) when this creature enters the battlefield, and (2) when the creature this card haunts dies. Both events are modelled as the single `EntersOrHauntedCreatureDies`. Must be tried before the generic enters/dies rules (higher priority) — this trigger contains both words.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/HauntCompoundConditionRule.cs)

### `InvestigateTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"investigate." — Rule 701.16a keyword action on the triggered side. Matches the bare keyword action when it appears as the entire effect body of a triggered ability (e.g. Thraben Inspector's ETB trigger). CR 701.16a: "Investigate" means "Create a Clue token." See rule 111.10f. CR 603.6a: Enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield. These are written, "When [this object] enters, . . . "... REUSE-ONLY: emits the existing `InvestigateEffect` node. No new AST types are introduced.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/InvestigateTriggeredRule.cs)

### `LookAtTopCardsTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"look at the top N cards of your library, then put them back in any order." — The Crystal Seer ETB pattern. Rule 701.12 (look) + unconditional reorder. The controller looks at the top N cards then returns all of them in any chosen order. Distinct from scry (top/bottom choice per card) and surveil (graveyard option).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/LookAtTopCardsTriggeredRule.cs)

### `LoseLifeDerivedRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"target opponent loses that much life" — Vito's drain shape. Encodes the derived quantity (LifeGained antecedent).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/LoseLifeDerivedRule.cs)

### `MillTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"mill N cards." / "mill a card." — Rule 701.17 keyword action on the triggered side. The implicit subject (controller) is encoded as Player = You. Also handles "target player mills N cards." (Constellation pattern such as Sage of Mysteries / Thassa's Devourer) where the target is encoded as Player = Target + player filter (Rule 115.1).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/MillTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ModifyPTTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it gets +N/+N until end of turn" — triggered P/T modification on the creature that fired the trigger (Rule 508 attack trigger pattern). The subject "it" refers to the attacking or otherwise-triggering creature and maps to `It`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ModifyPTTriggeredRule.cs)

### `MutatesConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever this creature mutates" — mutate trigger (Rule 702.140). Fires each time the creature successfully mutates. The Filter captures the subject ("this creature") as a creature-type filter.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/MutatesConditionRule.cs)

### `NamedSourceDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"[CardName] deals N damage to any target." — named-source self-ping triggered effect. Covers cards whose triggered ability effect reads "[CardName] deals N damage to any target." where the card refers to itself by its own printed name (Rule 201.4 — a card's name refers to itself when used in its own text). MAST resolves the self-reference to `Self`. Rule 120.1-120.2: dealing damage (a source deals damage to a permanent/player). Rule 115.4: "any target" — a target may be any player, creature, planeswalker, or battle unless otherwise restricted. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` (which handles the pronoun "it deals N damage" shape — same event, different oracle phrasing). Both map to Source=Self; the difference is naming convention only.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/NamedSourceDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `PhaseTriggerConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"At the beginning of your upkeep" / "...first main phase" / "...draw step" / "...end step" / "...combat on your turn". Maps the phase/step word to a `TriggerEvent`. The "your" / "each opponent's" possessive lands on the filter as a `Controller`. Only fires for At-timing triggers.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PhaseTriggerConditionRule.cs)

### `ProliferateTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"proliferate." — Rule 701.27 keyword action on the triggered side. Matches the bare proliferate instruction that appears after the trigger comma (reminder text has already been stripped by the time this rule is called).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ProliferateTriggeredRule.cs)

### `PutCountersTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"put a +1/+1 counter on this creature" / "put a -1/-1 counter on target creature" / "that player gets N [type] counters" (poison/energy/etc. given to a player on combat damage — Fynn, the Fangbearer; Blighted Agent; etc.).

CR 122.1: "A counter is a marker placed on an object or player that modifies its characteristics and/or interacts with a rule or effect." Poison counters are placed on players; the "gets" verb in oracle text means the player receives those counters. Distinct from "put a counter on [permanent]" only in subject (player vs. permanent) and verb ("gets" vs. "put … on"). MAST models both as `putCounters` effect — the counter placement is the same action regardless of verb.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/PutCountersTriggeredRule.cs)

### `RemoveCounterTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"remove a +1/+1 counter from it" / "remove a charge counter from it" / "remove a -1/-1 counter from this creature" as a triggered effect. Covers counter-removal as the primary triggered action, e.g.: "At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a -1/-1 counter from Aboroth." "At end of combat, remove a +1/+1 counter from it." "At the beginning of your upkeep, remove a charge counter from this artifact." Counter type resolution mirrors `PutCountersTriggeredRule`: "+1/+1" and "-1/-1" are matched directly; all other types are captured from the "a(n) &lt;type&gt; counter" pattern (Rule 122.1 — named counters). Target resolution order: 1. "from it" → ObjectReference.It() 2. "from this creature/permanent/artifact/enchantment" → ObjectReference.Self() 3. Named self-reference fallback (card name in text) → ObjectReference.Self()

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/RemoveCounterTriggeredRule.cs)

### `RemoveCountersTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"remove a +1/+1 counter from it at end of combat" — Phantom-creature counter-removal pattern (Judgment set). Fires as the resolution effect of an attacks-or-blocks triggered ability; "it" is the pronoun for the creature that declared as an attacker or blocker.

Rule 122.3: removing a counter from a permanent means taking one counter of the specified type off that permanent. Rule 511: the end of combat step is the last step of the combat phase; delayed removal effects scheduled "at end of combat" resolve here.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/RemoveCountersTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnEnchantedOnDeathTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return that card to the battlefield under your control" — the Animate Dead / Unhallowed Pact pattern. This effect appears as the resolution clause of a "When enchanted creature dies" trigger on Auras that reanimate their host. "That card" is a pronoun back-reference (Rule 113.8b) to the enchanted creature that just moved from the battlefield to the graveyard. MAST models it as `It` — the generic anaphoric reference kind ("it", "that card", "the creature" all denote the same game object). The "under your control" clause is captured in `UnderControl = You` on the `ReturnToBattlefieldEffect`. Rule 400.6: an object that returns to the battlefield under a specific player's control enters under that player's control as a replacement on zone-change resolution. Variant: "return it to its owner's hand" is handled by `ReturnSelfToHandOnDeathTriggeredRule` — the effect text is identical regardless of whether the trigger subject is "this Aura" or "enchanted creature", so no separate rule is needed for the hand-return variant. Rule 303.4 (Aura attachment); Rule 700.4 ("dies"); Rule 400.6 (enters under control).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnEnchantedOnDeathTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnInstantOrSorceryFromGraveyardRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return target instant or sorcery card from your graveyard to your hand" Models the Izzet Chronarch / Archaeomancer family (Rule 701.9: Return). The oracle text always targets a card by type-disjunction ("instant or sorcery") in the controller's graveyard and moves it to the controller's hand. Zone = Graveyard encodes the source zone stated in oracle text; Controller = You encodes "your graveyard". CardTypes = ["instant", "sorcery"] encodes the disjunction (Rule 700.4). Priorities above the generic ReturnToHandRule (default 50) because this rule is more specific (graveyard source + type-disjunction shape).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnInstantOrSorceryFromGraveyardRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfFromGraveyardTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may return this card from your graveyard to your hand" — graveyard-based self-retrieval triggered from the graveyard zone (Rule 603.6a: a triggered ability on a card in a graveyard functions from that zone). The Eidolon family (Dissension) is the canonical corpus for this pattern. The source zone (Graveyard) is encoded on the Target filter's Zone axis rather than on the ability itself — MAST describes what the oracle text says, not where the object resides at runtime (Rule 400 series). Example corpus patterns: "you may return this card from your graveyard to your hand"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnSelfFromGraveyardTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnSelfToHandOnDeathTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return it to its owner's hand" — self-referential bounce that appears on Aura (and occasionally other) cards whose death trigger returns themselves to hand. The pronoun "it" back-references the triggering object named in the trigger condition ("this Aura", "this artifact", etc.). There is no formal target (Rule 115.1 — "target" keyword absent); the card simply moves zone-change without targeting. Example corpus patterns: "return it to its owner's hand"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnSelfToHandOnDeathTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnThisToHandTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return this [type] to its owner's hand" — a self-referential bounce where the card names its own type word ("this enchantment", "this creature", "this artifact"...) to refer to itself. Resolves to `Self` — the card moving its own zone with no target (Rule 115.1 — "target" keyword absent, so no target is chosen). Higher priority than `ReturnToHandRule` so the self-reference "this [type]" is recognised as Self rather than mis-read as an indefinite "a [type] you control" choice (ObjectReferenceKind.Any). The "this [object]" phrasing is the oracle convention for a card referring to itself; modelling it as Self keeps the AST descriptively faithful to what the text says. Example corpus pattern: "return this enchantment to its owner's hand"

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnThisToHandTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ReturnToHandRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"return ... to its owner's hand" — type-disjunction, you-control, optional "you may"/"up to N", and non-prefix qualifiers (e.g. "nonland permanent", "nontoken creature").

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnToHandRule.cs)

### `ReturnToHandWithIfYouDoGainLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Niambi shape: "you may return ... to its owner's hand. If you do, you gain life equal to that creature's mana value." Priority 80: must override `ReturnToHandRule` so the IfYouDo continuation isn't dropped.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ReturnToHandWithIfYouDoGainLifeRule.cs)

### `SacrificeAnyCreatureTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice a creature" — triggered player-choice sacrifice. The controller picks any creature they control at resolution; there is no formal targeting declaration (Rule 115.1 — only the "target" keyword creates a target), so the reference kind is `Any`. Covers the classic upkeep-tax pattern used on cards like Necrite (FEM/ME2). Distinct from `SacrificeTriggeredRule`, which handles the pronoun forms "sacrifice it / this creature / this permanent" where the object to sacrifice is already determined by the trigger context.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeAnyCreatureTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SacrificeAtEndStepTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Recognises the delayed-sacrifice sentence within a triggered ability effect body:

"Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step."

This sentence appears as a trailing clause in multi-sentence triggered ability effects — typically after a token-creation effect. For example: "Whenever this creature attacks, create a 1/1 red Goblin creature token. Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step." The pronoun "it" refers to the token or creature created/obtained by the preceding clause (Rule 109.2 — game objects). MAST records this as a `SacrificeEffect` whose `Target` is `It` and whose `Duration` is `AtBeginningOfNextEndStepDuration`. Descriptive, not engine-executable (descriptive-not-engine doctrine). The trigger timing semantics are carried by the duration node. Priority 70: must supersede `SacrificeTriggeredRule` (plain "sacrifice it") so the more-specific duration-bearing form fires first. Rule citations: 701.21 (Sacrifice), 513 (End Step), 603.7 (delayed triggers).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeAtEndStepTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SacrificeSelfUnlessPayRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice this creature unless you pay {COST}" — the upkeep-tax pattern on creature cards (Rule 701.21a — Sacrifice; Rule 117.7 — unless clause).

Oracle text split by `TriggeredAbilityParser`: trigger = "At the beginning of your upkeep" effect = "sacrifice this creature unless you pay {B}"

The pronoun form "this creature" is a self-reference (Rule 109.2): the creature bearing the ability is the object that would be sacrificed. MAST models this as `Self`, distinct from the "sacrifice it" ETB-land pattern handled by `SacrificeUnlessPayTriggeredRule` which uses `It`.

Produces a `SacrificeEffect` with Target = Self and an `UnlessClause` whose Player is You and whose Cost is the parsed mana expression.

Representative cards: Whipstitched Zombie (NEM), Wild Leotau (CON), Spindrift Drake (WTH), Molting Harpy (UDS). Rule citations: 701.21 (Sacrifice), 117.7 (unless clause).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeSelfUnlessPayRule.cs)

### `SacrificeTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice it" — triggered self-sacrifice on the creature that fired the trigger (Rule 701.21a — Sacrifice). The pronoun "it" refers back to the trigger subject and maps to `It`, matching the pronoun- reference convention used elsewhere in triggered rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SacrificeUnlessPayTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"sacrifice it unless you pay {COST}" — the Karoo/bounce-land ETB sacrifice pattern (Rule 701.21a — Sacrifice; Rule 117.7 — unless clause).

Oracle text split by `TriggeredAbilityParser`: trigger = "When this land enters" effect = "sacrifice it unless you pay {1}"

Produces a `SacrificeEffect` with Target = It and an `UnlessClause` whose Player is You and whose Cost is the parsed mana expression. "It" refers back to the land named as the trigger subject — matching the pronoun-reference convention used in `SacrificeTriggeredRule`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SacrificeUnlessPayTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ScryOrSurveilConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you scry or surveil" — fires on either keyword action (Rule 701.18 / 701.43).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ScryOrSurveilConditionRule.cs)

### `ScryTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"scry N." — Rule 701.18 keyword action on the triggered side.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ScryTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SearchBasicLandTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may search your library for a basic land card, [reveal it,] put [it|that card] onto the battlefield tapped / into your hand, then shuffle." Covers the ETB-search-land pattern (Rule 701.23: Search). Two destination variants: - put it into your hand (Destination=Hand, Revealed=true) - put that card onto the battlefield tapped (Destination=BattlefieldTapped, Revealed=false)

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SearchBasicLandTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SearchForNamedCardTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may search your library [and/or graveyard] for a card named [Name], reveal it, [and] put it into your hand. [If you search your library this way, shuffle.]" Covers the ETB named-card tutor pattern (Rule 701.23: Search), e.g. Sorin's Guide. Distinct from `SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule` in two ways the older rule cannot express: 1. The search target is pinned by exact name ("a card named X") rather than a card-type/subtype category — recorded on `Name`. 2. The search may span multiple source zones ("library and/or graveyard") — recorded on `Sources`. A library-only search leaves Sources null (the default Rule 701.23 case). The "you may" prefix is optional; its presence sets IsOptional=true. The reveal clause sets Revealed=true. The trailing conditional-shuffle sentence ("If you search your library this way, shuffle.") is rules-inferred bookkeeping and is folded into the effect rather than modelled separately — the same convention SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule uses for its "then shuffle" tail. Priority 70 (above SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule's 60) so the named/ multi-zone shape is recognised before the generic category-tutor rule.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SearchForNamedCardTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you may search your library for a [filter] card, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle." Handles the non-basic-land ETB tutor pattern (Rule 701.23: Search). Supports three filter shapes: 1. Plain card type — "a creature card", "an artifact card", etc. 2. Subtype (tribal) — "an Elf card", "a Goblin card", etc. 3. Card type + mana value comparison — "a creature card with mana value 6 or greater" The "you may" prefix is optional; its presence sets IsOptional=true. Only the Hand destination is covered here (reveal → hand → shuffle). The SearchBasicLandTriggeredRule handles basic-land searches at higher priority. Priority 60 (above default 50) so this is tried before generic fallback rules but after any higher-priority specialised rules.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SearchLibraryToHandTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals N damage to any target." — ETB self-ping targeting any legal target (creature, player, or planeswalker). Covers cards whose triggered ETB ability reads "When this [creature|artifact] enters, it deals N damage to any target." Rule 603: triggered abilities (When/Whenever/At). Rule 120.1-120.2: dealing damage (a source deals damage to a permanent/player). Rule 115.4: "any target" — a target may be any player, creature, planeswalker, or battle unless otherwise restricted. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToYouRule` (targets the controller only) and the spell-side `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetRule` (subject is the spell itself, not a triggered pronoun).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToOpponentRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals N damage to target opponent." — ETB self-ping targeting a single opponent. Covers cards whose triggered ETB ability reads "When this [land|creature|artifact] enters, it deals N damage to target opponent." Rule 603: triggered abilities (When/Whenever/At). Rule 120.1-120.2: dealing damage (a source deals damage to a player). Rule 115.1: "target" creates a target. Rule 102.2: an opponent is a player not on the same team as the controller. Distinct from `SelfDealsDamageToAnyTargetTriggeredRule` (which targets any legal target — creature, player, or planeswalker — per Rule 115.4) and from `SelfDealsDamageToYouRule` (which targets the controller).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToOpponentRule.cs)

### `SelfDealsDamageToYouRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"it deals N damage to you" / "this creature deals N damage to you" — reflexive damage.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SelfDealsDamageToYouRule.cs)

### `SpellCastConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"Whenever you cast a spell" / "Whenever an opponent casts a spell" / "Whenever you cast a spell that's white, blue, black, or red" / etc. Encodes the caster and any inline spell-color/type qualifiers on the filter.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SpellCastConditionRule.cs)

### `SubtypeEntersConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"a [Subtype] you control enters" — a subtype-filtered enters trigger keyed on the indefinite article "a"/"an" rather than "another". The triggering object is some OTHER permanent (not this card) that shares the named subtype; the subtype is the most precise type word the oracle text gives, so the filter carries it on the Subtypes axis with no CardTypes constraint. Rule 603.6a: enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield, written "When [this object] enters, ..." or "Whenever a [type] enters, ...". Rule 603.2: when a game event matches a triggered ability's trigger event, that ability automatically triggers. This is distinct from a this-object ETB trigger (handled by `EntersConditionRule` via the "this [type]" self-reference) and from the creature-subtype "another [Subtype] you control enters" shape (`AnotherSubtypeEntersConditionRule`, which keys on "another" and pins CardTypes to creature). Here the subtype may be any permanent subtype — e.g. the Aura subtype Cartouche — so no card-type is asserted. Examples: "When a Cartouche you control enters, ..." Sits just below `EntersConditionRule` (Priority 990) so the generic enters recogniser gets first refusal on plainer shapes ("a creature you control enters", "this enchantment enters"); this rule only fires once that generic recogniser has declined, then narrows on the proper- noun subtype.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SubtypeEntersConditionRule.cs)

### `SupportTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"support N" — Rule 701.41 keyword action on the triggered side.

Rule 701.41a: "\"Support N\" on a permanent means \"Put a +1/+1 counter on each of up to N other target creatures.\"" This rule covers the permanent form (the "other" variant); a permanent's "support N" never targets itself.

The keyword action is unpacked into its literal meaning per the rule (MAST describes what the keyword says, not how it resolves): a single +1/+1 counter placed on each of an up-to-N choice of OTHER target creatures. The "up to N" is the multiplicity of the target reference (Rule 115.1 — "up to N target"), not the per-target counter count, which is one. Hence `Count` is the literal 1 and the "up to N" lives on the reference's `Quantity`.

The reminder text "(Put a +1/+1 counter on each of up to two other target creatures.)" has already been stripped by the dispatcher before this rule is called, so the matched fragment is the bare "support N".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SupportTriggeredRule.cs)

### `SurveilTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"surveil N." and "you may surveil N." — Rule 701.42 keyword action on the triggered side. The "you may" prefix produces an optional `SurveilEffect` (IsOptional = true).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/SurveilTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TakeInitiativeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you take the initiative." — Rule 726. The player takes the initiative game-state designation. Parameterless; mirrors AscendEffect doctrine.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TakeInitiativeRule.cs)

### `TapAndFreezeBackReferenceTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Single-sentence "tap then freeze" back-reference effect, as it appears on the resolution half of a triggered ability: "tap that creature and it doesn't untap during its controller's next untap step." (Kashi-Tribe Warriors — the combat-damage-to-a-creature trigger family).

Two conjoined instructions on one sentence: a `TapEffect` on the back-referenced creature ("that creature") and a `DoesntUntapEffect` on that same creature ("it"). Both back-references resolve to `It` — the creature the trigger condition just named (the one dealt combat damage). The pair is wrapped in a single `CompositeEffect`, matching the single-sentence-conjunction convention already used on the triggered side (`EtbTeamPumpTriggeredRule` for "get +N/+M and gain &lt;keyword&gt;").

CR 502.3 (Untap Step): "Third, the active player determines which permanents they control will untap. Then they untap them all simultaneously... Normally, all of a player's permanents untap, but effects can keep one or more of a player's permanents from untapping." The "next untap step" wording is recorded on `WhoseUntapStep` as "its controller's next".

Mirrors `TapAndFreezeRule` (the two-sentence spell form, "Tap target creature. It doesn't untap...") but covers the one-sentence "and"-joined back-reference form that surfaces in triggered-ability resolution text, where the creature is not re-targeted.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TapAndFreezeBackReferenceTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TapEnchantedTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"tap enchanted [type]" — Aura enters-the-battlefield tap effect. Fires when an Aura's triggered ability instructs it to tap the enchanted permanent. The target is always the permanent the Aura is attached to, modelled as `EnchantedOrEquipped`. Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap); Rule 303.4 (Aura attachment).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TapEnchantedTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TapUntapTargetTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Triggered-ability rule for tap, untap, and tap-or-untap effects on a named target. Covers three surface patterns:

"tap target [filter]" → `TapEffect` "untap target [filter]" → `UntapEffect` "(you may) tap or untap target [filter]" → `TapOrUntapEffect`

The filter phrase may be a single card type ("creature") or a disjunction ("artifact or creature", "creature or land", "artifact, creature, or land"). Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TapUntapTargetTriggeredRule.cs)

### `ThatPlayerLosesLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"that player loses N life" — symmetry-drain trigger on an opponent-controlled-ETB trigger (Suture Priest pattern). "That player" is the pronoun for the player whose action fired the trigger (Rule 109.5) and maps to `ThatPlayer`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/ThatPlayerLosesLifeRule.cs)

### `TransferCountersOnDeathTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"put its counters on target creature you control" — counter-transfer shape that moves all counters from the dying creature to a target controlled creature. The phrase "its counters" is an anaphoric reference to the triggering creature's current counter set (all types and amounts); MAST describes this as `CounterType = "all"` (the sentinel for "every counter type present on the source") with a `DerivedQuantity` source of "its", meaning the count is whatever is on the dying permanent rather than a fixed number (Rule 122.1 — counters are game objects; their quantity is a game state). Oracle text shape (after trigger/effect split): "put its counters on target creature you control" Example: "When Goobling dies, put its counters on target creature you control." (ONE) Priority 60: more specific than the generic `PutCountersTriggeredRule` (priority 50) which requires an explicit counter type ("a +1/+1 counter", "a charge counter", etc.) and would fall through on "its counters" anyway. The higher priority ensures this rule is tried first for the "its counters" surface without ambiguity.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TransferCountersOnDeathTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TransformTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

Matches "transform this creature" / "transform this permanent" effect text. Classic Innistrad werewolves carry explicit transform triggered abilities, e.g.:

"At the beginning of each upkeep, if a player cast two or more spells last turn, transform this creature." (human-to-wolf) "At the beginning of each upkeep, if no spells were cast last turn, transform this creature." (wolf-to-human)

The "if [condition]" intervening-if clause is stripped by `TriggeredAbilityParser` before this rule receives the effect fragment. This rule handles the remaining "transform this creature" / "transform this permanent" text.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TransformTriggeredRule.cs)

### `TurnedFaceUpConditionRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggerConditionRule

"When this creature is turned face up" — Rule 702.37 (Morph/Megamorph) face-up trigger. Fires when the face-down permanent's morph or megamorph cost is paid and the card becomes face up (Rule 702.37e/f). Subject is always "this creature".

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/TurnedFaceUpConditionRule.cs)

### `UntapAllTriggeredRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"untap all [types] you control" — mass-untap triggered effect. Covers patterns such as:

"untap all lands you control" (Bear Umbra) "untap all creatures you control" "untap all permanents you control"

Produces an `UntapEffect` whose Target is `Each` filtered to the named card type with `You` when "you control" is present, or no controller filter otherwise (e.g. "untap all creatures"). Rule 701.26 (Tap and Untap).

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/UntapAllTriggeredRule.cs)

### `UntapSelfRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"untap this artifact" / "untap it" with optional "you may pay {X}. If you do, ..." gate.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/UntapSelfRule.cs)

### `YouGainLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you gain N life" — ETB lifegain trigger effect. Rule 701.20: "To gain life, a player adds the indicated amount to their life total."

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouGainLifeRule.cs)

### `YouGainThatMuchLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you gain that much life" — lifelink-analog triggered effect where the amount is derived from the damage dealt by the source in the triggering event. Rule 120 (Damage): a source deals damage; the amount is "that much" referencing the damage dealt in the triggering event. Rule 701.20: "To gain life, a player adds the indicated amount to their life total." Distinct from `YouGainLifeRule` (literal N life) — this rule models a derived amount ("that much") backed by `DamageDealt`.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouGainThatMuchLifeRule.cs)

### `YouLoseLifeRule` — *sealed class* : ITriggeredRule

"you lose N life" — Deadpool's upkeep tax shape.

[Source](Parsing/Parsers/Triggered/Rules/YouLoseLifeRule.cs)

## `/Parsing/Tokens/`

### `OracleTokenizer` — *sealed class* : Tokenizer<OracleToken>

Tokenizer for Magic: The Gathering oracle text. Transforms character streams into token streams for parsing. Structural keywords are discovered via reflection at startup from classes decorated with `StructuralKeywordAttribute`. To add a new keyword, create a new file under `Parsing/Tokens/Keywords/`; no edits to this file are required.

[Source](Parsing/Tokens/OracleTokenizer.cs)

### `OracleToken` — *enum*

Token types recognized in Magic: The Gathering oracle text. These represent the lexical units that the tokenizer produces.

[Source](Parsing/Tokens/OracleToken.cs)

## `/Parsing/Tokens/Keywords/`

### `StructuralKeywordAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute

Marks a class as the registration site for a structural keyword recognized by `OracleTokenizer`. The class body is conventionally empty — the attribute is the entire registration. Each tagged class is discovered at runtime by `StructuralKeywordRegistry`.

[Source](Parsing/Tokens/Keywords/StructuralKeywordAttribute.cs)

### `StructuralKeywordRegistry` — *static class*

Discovers all `StructuralKeywordAttribute`-tagged types in the MagicAST assembly and exposes a case-insensitive text -> token lookup. Discovery runs once on first access and is cached for the process lifetime. Adding a new structural keyword means creating a new file decorated with `StructuralKeywordAttribute` — no edits to this registry or to `OracleTokenizer` are required.

[Source](Parsing/Tokens/Keywords/StructuralKeywordRegistry.cs)

## `/Serialization/`

### `PolymorphicTypeAttribute` — *abstract class* : Attribute

Abstract base for attributes that register a derived concrete type into a polymorphic AST hierarchy. Each polymorphic base has its own concrete subclass (e.g., `OracleAbilityAttribute`, `OracleEffectAttribute`) so that the attribute name signals which hierarchy the type belongs to. Discovered at startup by `PolymorphicReflectionConverter{TBase}`.

[Source](Serialization/PolymorphicTypeAttribute.cs)

### `PolymorphicBaseAttribute` — *sealed class* : Attribute

Marks an abstract record/class as the root of a polymorphic AST hierarchy serialized via `PolymorphicReflectionConverter{TBase}`. The named discriminator property is injected by the converter on write and consumed (then stripped) by the converter on read. The discriminator value itself is supplied per-derived-type by a `PolymorphicTypeAttribute` (or one of its subclasses such as `OracleAbilityAttribute`).

[Source](Serialization/PolymorphicBaseAttribute.cs)

### `PolymorphicConverterFactory` — *sealed class* : JsonConverterFactory

A `JsonConverterFactory` that produces a `PolymorphicReflectionConverter{TBase}` for any type marked with `PolymorphicBaseAttribute`. The set of recognised base types is discovered at startup by scanning the MagicAST assembly. Adding a new polymorphic hierarchy means:

Decorate the abstract base with `PolymorphicBaseAttribute`. Author a per-base `PolymorphicTypeAttribute` subclass (e.g., `OracleAbilityAttribute`). Decorate concrete derived types with that attribute.

No edits to this factory, to `MagicASTJsonOptions`, or to any existing base class are required.

[Source](Serialization/PolymorphicConverterFactory.cs)
